87: // vocabulary K advertises, and `certify` is no longer part of it.
88: export const TRANSITIONAL_LIFECYCLE_VERBS = ['certify'] as const;
89: 
90: // What the flat-anchor path actually ACCEPTS today: the canonical vocabulary
91: // plus whatever alias is still in its deploy window. The OpenAPI document is
92: // generated from this same tuple (anchoring.openapi.ts) so the published
93: // contract never claims to reject a value the server accepts — a spec that is
94: // stricter than its server is a spec that lies, and the fuzzer reports it as a
95: // negative_data_rejection failure.
96: export const ACCEPTED_LIFECYCLE_VERBS = [
97:   ...LIFECYCLE_VERBS,
98:   ...TRANSITIONAL_LIFECYCLE_VERBS,
99: ] as const;
100: 
101: // ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
102: // KS-1175 (Stuart 2026-09-16, PS-869 ruling; Kam's ruling 2026-09-20 19:42:44 +10:00 "remove the rule and make the
103: // fix"): Platform-S's NON-PII actor + organisation identity block, sent TOP-LEVEL as `identity` on every anchored
104: // write (the wire shape S BUILT behind `CardanoAnchor:AnchorIdentity`, PS-870 — not the `metadata.identity` the
105: // ticket description floated). K anchors the seven readable fields FLAT under `secuura`, beside documentType /
106: // occurredAt / identityCommitment, so S can render "on chain: Level 2, entra-verified-id" next to its local
107: // snapshot and flag a mismatch (PS-871).
108: //
109: // Non-PII by the ticket's own definition, and admitted under KS-240 on the SAME argument as identityCommitment
110: // (the identityCommitment comment inside `metadata` below): K proves SHAPE by regex/enum/type and never SEMANTICS. DIDs are pseudonymous identifiers, levels
111: // / sources / trust are closed enumerations, the verified flags are booleans — no free text, no dates, no names, no
112: // emails. That the values are non-identifying is Platform-S's assertion, recorded on PS-869 / PS-711, not a property
113: // K can prove. Posture note, not a guard: `did:web:<domain>` is admitted by the regex and a domain can name a person;
114: // the ticket calls it non-PII and the erasure story is S's.
115: // KS-1175 / PS-1324 (Stuart's ruling, 2026-10-22): `did:jwk:<base64url JWK>` is admitted on the same footing. It encodes
116: // a public key; K checks its shape (prefix, alphabet, cap). The pattern was `key|web` before. One narrow exception to
117: // "shape only", Kam's ruling of 2026-10-29 (didJwkRefusal below): a did:jwk is decoded far enough to see its member
118: // NAMES, and one that carries a private-key member, or does not decode to a JSON object, is a caller error (400).
119: //
120: // Shape rules: every field optional (S omits absent fields, never sends null / ""); a PRESENT value that is malformed
121: // — actorLevel 4, actorSource 'google', a 513-char DID, actorVerified 'yes', null, "" — is a caller error (400),
122: // never silently anchored (the documentType / timestamp posture inside `metadata`); unknown keys INSIDE `identity`
123: // (actorName, actorEmail, …) are stripped like everywhere else here (KS-240).
124: export const DID_PATTERN = /^did:(key|web|jwk):[A-Za-z0-9._:%-]+$/; // KS-1175 / PS-1324: + jwk (was key|web); cap unchanged
125: export const DID_MAX_LENGTH = 512;
126: export const ACTOR_LEVELS = [1, 2, 3] as const; // NIST 800-63A IAL number, not the S-internal enum
127: export const ACTOR_SOURCES = ['platform', 'stripe', 'entra-verified-id'] as const;
128: export const ORG_TRUST_LEVELS = ['unverified', 'domain', 'document', 'kyc', 'government'] as const;
129: // KS-721's commitment shape, shared by metadata.identityCommitment and identity.identityCommitment.
130: export const IDENTITY_COMMITMENT_PATTERN = /^[a-z0-9-]+\.v\d+:[0-9a-f]{64}$/;
131: export const IDENTITY_COMMITMENT_MAX_LENGTH = 128;
132: /** The seven readable identity fields, in the order they are anchored. identityCommitment is NOT one of them —
133:  *  it keeps its own key (KS-721) whichever of the two request positions it arrived in. */
134: export const IDENTITY_FIELDS = [
135:   'actorDid',
136:   'actorLevel',
137:   'actorSource',
138:   'actorVerified',
139:   'orgDid',
140:   'orgTrust',
141:   'orgVerified',
142: ] as const;
143: 
144: // KS-1175 (Kam's ruling, 2026-10-29 19:51:25 AEDT, card secuura-ks1175-private-jwk-guard-1029, option a): a did:jwk is a
145: // PUBLIC key by construction, and whatever is anchored is public and permanent, so a JWK that carries private-key
146: // material (RFC 7518 / RFC 8037: `d` for EC / OKP / RSA, `p` `q` `dp` `dq` `qi` for RSA, `k` for oct) must never reach the chain.
147: // The shape pattern cannot see inside the payload, so this decodes it, ONLY far enough to read the member NAMES. Nothing
148: // else about the JWK is checked: no kty / crv / key use, no key length, no signature. Members are matched by exact name at
149: // the top level (the JWK's own members); a value that happens to read "d", a nested object, or a differently-cased name is not
150: // a member of that name and is not refused.
151: export const DID_JWK_PRIVATE_MEMBERS = ['d', 'p', 'q', 'dp', 'dq', 'qi', 'k'] as const;
152: const DID_JWK_PREFIX = 'did:jwk:';
153: const BASE64URL_PAYLOAD = /^[A-Za-z0-9_-]+$/;
154: 
155: /**
156:  * The refusal message for a did:jwk that is not a public key, or null when it is one (or is not a did:jwk at all).
157:  * A value the shape checks already refuse (over the cap, outside the alphabet) is left to those checks, so a bad value
158:  * is refused once, for its own reason.
159:  *
160:  * Strict by design: the payload must be unpadded base64url text BEFORE it is decoded, because Node's decoder skips any
161:  * other character without complaint (`.`, `:` and `%` pass the DID pattern), so a lenient decode could read a
162:  * different JWK from the one a strict resolver sees. A payload that is not base64url, not JSON, or not a JSON object
163:  * is refused: an undecodable did:jwk is not a public key. The message names the offending members, never a value.
164:  */
165: function didJwkRefusal(value: string): string | null {
166:   if (!value.startsWith(DID_JWK_PREFIX) || value.length > DID_MAX_LENGTH || !DID_PATTERN.test(value)) return null;
167:   const payload = value.slice(DID_JWK_PREFIX.length);
168:   let jwk: unknown;
169:   try {
170:     if (!BASE64URL_PAYLOAD.test(payload)) throw new Error('not base64url');
171:     jwk = JSON.parse(Buffer.from(payload, 'base64url').toString('utf8'));
172:   } catch {
173:     return 'Invalid DID — a did:jwk must be unpadded base64url of a JSON object (a public JWK)';
174:   }
175:   if (typeof jwk !== 'object' || jwk === null || Array.isArray(jwk)) {
176:     return 'Invalid DID — a did:jwk must be unpadded base64url of a JSON object (a public JWK)';
177:   }
178:   const names = Object.keys(jwk);
179:   const present = DID_JWK_PRIVATE_MEMBERS.filter((member) => names.includes(member));
180:   return present.length === 0
181:     ? null
182:     : `Invalid DID — a did:jwk must carry a public key only; private-key member(s) present: ${present.join(', ')}`;
183: }
184: 
185: // KS-1175 / PS-1324: the refusal message names did:jwk too (was 'expect did:key:… or did:web:…'). It must keep starting
186: // with "Invalid DID": originate's ks1709 refused-anchor logging tests quote that prefix.
187: const didField = z
188:   .string()
189:   .max(DID_MAX_LENGTH)
190:   .regex(DID_PATTERN, 'Invalid DID — expect did:key:…, did:web:… or did:jwk:…')
191:   .superRefine((value, ctx) => {
192:     const refusal = didJwkRefusal(value);
193:     if (refusal !== null) ctx.addIssue({ code: z.ZodIssueCode.custom, message: refusal });
194:   });
195: 
196: export const identitySchema = z.object({
197:   actorDid: didField.optional(),
198:   // z.number().int() would admit 0 and 4; the closed set is the contract (PS-869 table).
199:   actorLevel: z.union([z.literal(1), z.literal(2), z.literal(3)]).optional(),
200:   actorSource: z.enum(ACTOR_SOURCES).optional(),
201:   actorVerified: z.boolean().optional(),
202:   orgDid: didField.optional(),
203:   orgTrust: z.enum(ORG_TRUST_LEVELS).optional(),
204:   orgVerified: z.boolean().optional(),
205:   identityCommitment: z.string().max(IDENTITY_COMMITMENT_MAX_LENGTH).regex(
206:     IDENTITY_COMMITMENT_PATTERN,
207:     'Invalid identityCommitment format — expect <label>.v<N>:<64 lowercase hex>',
208:   ).optional(),
209: });
210: 
211: export type AnchorIdentity = z.infer<typeof identitySchema>;
212: 
213: export const anchorDocumentSchema = z.object({
214:   documentId: z.string().min(1).max(128).regex(
215:     /^(doc-\d+-[A-Za-z0-9_-]+|[0-9a-fA-F]{8}-[0-9a-fA-F]{4}-[0-9a-fA-F]{4}-[0-9a-fA-F]{4}-[0-9a-fA-F]{12})$/,
216:     'Invalid documentId format',
217:   ),
218:   contentHash: z.string().regex(/^[a-f0-9]{64}$/i, 'Invalid hash format'),
219:   environment: z.string().min(1).max(32).optional(),
220:   issuerOrgId: z.string().min(1).max(128).optional(),
221:   // KS-240: accepted for backward compatibility but NO LONGER anchored on-chain
222:   // (resolved via marry-up from the platform DB). Strict on-chain minimisation.
223:   issuerName: z.string().min(1).max(256).optional(),
224:   certId: z.string().min(1).max(128).optional(),
225:   // KS-240: metadata anchored on-chain is restricted to non-identifying fields.
226:   // issuerId / ownerId were party identifiers and are no longer accepted here
227:   // (strict on-chain minimisation). zod strips any extra keys a caller sends.
228:   metadata: z.object({
229:     // KS-388: frozen to the KS-134 master verb vocabulary (see LIFECYCLE_VERBS
230:     // above). An out-of-vocabulary verb is a caller error (400), never silently
231:     // anchored. K's own internal anchor calls send no metadata.documentType, so
232:     // this constrains only external flat-anchor callers (Platform-S).
233:     // KS-661: ACCEPTED_LIFECYCLE_VERBS = canonical + the transitional `certify`
234:     // alias. Widening for the alias must not weaken the guard — a verb outside
235:     // BOTH tuples still 400s (unit-tested with an explicit positive control).
236:     documentType: z.enum(ACCEPTED_LIFECYCLE_VERBS).optional(),
237:     // KS-555: the CLIENT-ASSERTED event time ("when it happened" on S's side),
238:     // anchored as `occurredAt` alongside K's own anchoring `timestamp` — after
239:     // a drain/replay, anchoring time alone misdates every replayed event.
240:     // Validated as ISO-8601 (offset allowed) because it reaches the immutable
241:     // public ledger: a garbage string is a caller error (400), never silently
242:     // anchored — same posture as the documentType vocabulary above (KS-388).
243:     timestamp: z.string().datetime({ offset: true }).optional(),
244:     // KS-721 (Stuart 2026-08-29; Kam's ruling 2026-08-31): Platform-S's keyed
245:     // identity commitment — HMAC-SHA256 over an actor's identity snapshot bound
246:     // to the event id, in the form `<label>.v<N>:<64 lowercase hex>` where `vN`
247:     // names the S-SIDE KEY VERSION so rotation never orphans an anchored value.
248:     //
249:     // KS-240 admits it as an opaque token, but with a condition K cannot itself
250:     // enforce and must not pretend to: a regex proves SHAPE, never SEMANTICS.
251:     // K accepts S's word that the value is non-identifying. What makes that
252:     // survivable is the crypto-shred commitment recorded on KS-721 — S destroys
253:     // the `vN` key version on erasure, so an anchored commitment becomes
254:     // permanently unverifiable rather than a permanent checkable pseudonym.
255:     // That commitment is the DEPLOY GATE for this field; it is not code.
256:     //
257:     // Anchored VERBATIM (see the payload builder below): normalising an opaque
258:     // token would change the value S can verify against. Lowercase hex only, for
259:     // the same reason KS-201 normalises `occurredAt` — the on-chain form has to
260:     // be deterministic, and two casings of one commitment are two anchors.
261:     identityCommitment: z.string().max(IDENTITY_COMMITMENT_MAX_LENGTH).regex(
262:       IDENTITY_COMMITMENT_PATTERN,
263:       'Invalid identityCommitment format — expect <label>.v<N>:<64 lowercase hex>',
264:     ).optional(),
265:     // KS-1454 (Stuart 2026-10-09, amended 10 Oct): the plain SHA-256 of the detached .p7s, lowercase hex, sign verb only (the refinement below refuses it on any other verb).
266:     signatureHash: z.string().regex(/^[a-f0-9]{64}$/, 'Invalid signatureHash format - expect 64 lowercase hex (the SHA-256 of the detached .p7s)').optional(),
267:   }).optional(),
268:   // KS-1175: the top-level identity block (see identitySchema above). On POST /api/anchors S sends the commitment
269:   // here AND as metadata.identityCommitment (KS-721's field) — the refinement below the object rules on the pair.
270:   identity: identitySchema.optional(),
271:   // KS-566: `onBehalfOf` is GONE from this surface. Kam's 2026-08-05 G-1
272:   // ruling (KS-539) puts the flat anchor on the CONNECTOR pattern — the
273:   // operation is attributed to `connector:<id>`, never to a named principal.
274:   //
275:   // IGNORED, NOT REJECTED, and deliberately so: Platform S is live sending
276:   // this field today (PS-616, deployed to UAT 2026-08-21T10:07Z), so a 400
277:   // would turn a K deploy into an S outage on one of their two
278:   // highest-traffic routes. Dropping the declaration lets zod's default
279:   // `.strip()` discard the key — which is what an undeclared field means
280:   // everywhere else here. Tightening to a 400 stays available under its own
281:   // ticket once S stops sending it.
282:   //
283:   // Attribution is NOT lost by the removal: index.ts now writes a
284:   // connector-only action_provenance row on every connector flat anchor, so
285:   // the row count per operation stays 1:1 (KS-566 fallback).
286: }).superRefine((data, ctx) => {
287:   // KS-1175 (DECIDE 2): identity.identityCommitment and metadata.identityCommitment may both arrive (S sends both on
288:   // POST /api/anchors). Equal, or only one present → exactly ONE identityCommitment key is anchored, where it is
289:   // today. Both present and DIFFERENT → 400: two values of one commitment are two anchors, and the on-chain form has
290:   // to be deterministic (the KS-721 "two casings of one commitment are two anchors" rule). Never silently pick one.
291:   const a = data.identity?.identityCommitment;
292:   const b = data.metadata?.identityCommitment;
293:   if (a !== undefined && b !== undefined && a !== b) {
294:     ctx.addIssue({
295:       code: z.ZodIssueCode.custom,
296:       path: ['identity', 'identityCommitment'],
297:       message: 'identity.identityCommitment and metadata.identityCommitment differ — send one value, or the same value in both',
298:     });
299:   }
300:   // KS-1454: a signature hash on any verb but `sign` (or with no verb) is a caller error (400); a stray hash must not be anchored as if it named a signature.
301:   if (data.metadata?.signatureHash !== undefined && data.metadata.documentType !== 'sign') {
302:     ctx.addIssue({
303:       code: z.ZodIssueCode.custom,
304:       path: ['metadata', 'signatureHash'],
305:       message: 'metadata.signatureHash is only accepted with metadata.documentType "sign"',
306:     });
307:   }
308: });
309: 
310: export type FlatAnchorRequest = z.infer<typeof anchorDocumentSchema>;
311: 
312: /**
313:  * KS-1384 F-a (design M5): the anchoring time K stamped into the payload a row will anchor or has anchored,
314:  * `metadata_payload.secuura.timestamp`, the same value that goes on chain. No column: it is read from the
315:  * stored payload. Originate's later resolver uses it to tell a document's own create from a later event that
316:  * shares the row (one row per document until per-event anchors). null when the payload carries none.
317:  */
318: export function payloadTimestampOf(metadataPayload: unknown): string | null {
319:   const sec = (metadataPayload as { secuura?: unknown } | null | undefined)?.secuura;
320:   if (!sec || typeof sec !== 'object') return null;
321:   const ts = (sec as Record<string, unknown>).timestamp;
322:   return typeof ts === 'string' ? ts : null;
323: }
324: 
325: /** KS-1175: the one commitment to anchor, whichever request position carried it (both agree by the refinement). */
326: export function resolveIdentityCommitment(data: FlatAnchorRequest): string | undefined {
327:   return data.metadata?.identityCommitment ?? data.identity?.identityCommitment;
328: }
329: 
330: /**
331:  * KS-240 / KS-480 §6 — THE single anchor-payload construction path for the
332:  * flat anchor. A fixed-field WHITELIST: nothing a caller sends reaches the
333:  * public ledger unless named here, so party identifiers and the onBehalfOf
334:  * provenance triplet are excluded STRUCTURALLY, not by per-field remembering.
335:  * Unit-tested (ks480-provenance-exclusion.test.ts) as the regression Peter's
336:  * §6 condition 1 requires. Do NOT add object spreads of caller input here.
337:  */
338: export function buildFlatAnchorMetadataPayload(
339:   data: FlatAnchorRequest,
340:   cardanoNetwork: string,
341:   // KS-1384 F-a (design M5, cell w): the route passes the ONE Date it also stores as the row's created_at, so
342:   // the anchored timestamp, the 202's payloadTimestamp and created_at on insert are the same instant.
343:   now: Date = new Date(),
344: ): { secuura: Record<string, unknown> } {
345:   return {
346:     secuura: {
347:       version: '2.0',
348:       type: 'document',
349:       hash: data.contentHash,
350:       algorithm: 'sha256',
351:       documentId: data.documentId,
352:       certId: data.certId || data.documentId,
353:       timestamp: now.toISOString(),
354:       network: cardanoNetwork,
355:       ...(data.environment ? { environment: data.environment } : {}),
356:       ...(data.issuerOrgId ? { issuerOrgId: data.issuerOrgId } : {}),
357:       // KS-240: issuerName is NOT anchored — strict on-chain minimisation
358:       // (only opaque tokens/hashes go on-chain); the human-readable name is
359:       // resolved at verify/display time from the platform DB so erasure
360:       // orphans it. Only the non-PII document category is whitelisted from
361:       // caller metadata.
362:       ...(data.metadata?.documentType ? { documentType: data.metadata.documentType } : {}),
363:       // KS-555: client-ASSERTED event time, distinct from `timestamp` above
364:       // (K-asserted anchoring time) — provenance carries both, labelled, no
365:       // trust conflation. Normalised to UTC ISO so the on-chain form is
366:       // deterministic and offset-independent (KS-201).
367:       ...(data.metadata?.timestamp
368:         ? { occurredAt: new Date(data.metadata.timestamp).toISOString() }
369:         : {}),
370:       // KS-721: S's opaque identity commitment, anchored VERBATIM — unlike
371:       // `occurredAt` there is nothing to normalise, and re-encoding an opaque
372:       // token would break the equality check S performs against it. Whitelisted
373:       // by name, like every other field here; the exact-key-set tests in
374:       // __tests__/anchorSchema.test.ts fail if anything joins this object
375:       // silently, which is the guard this widening earns.
376:       // KS-1175: the same key, now fed from either request position (identity.identityCommitment is S's
377:       // top-level carrier; metadata.identityCommitment is KS-721's) — one value, one key, resolved above.
378:       ...(resolveIdentityCommitment(data)
379:         ? { identityCommitment: resolveIdentityCommitment(data) }
380:         : {}),
381:       // KS-1454: the plain SHA-256 of S's detached .p7s, anchored VERBATIM (64 lowercase hex = exactly 64 bytes, the Cardano string cap, so the KS-1284 codec leaves it a single string).
382:       ...(data.metadata?.signatureHash ? { signatureHash: data.metadata.signatureHash } : {}),
383:       // KS-1175: the seven non-PII identity fields, FLAT under secuura and whitelisted BY NAME — seven explicit
384:       // spreads, never `...data.identity` (the whitelist rule in this function's doc-comment). Flat rather than a nested `secuura.identity` map
385:       // (DECIDE 3) so every anchored value stays a scalar: the KS-1284 chain codec treats them uniformly and the
386:       // exact-key-set cells keep naming every key that reaches the ledger. `!== undefined`, not truthiness — a
387:       // FALSE verified flag is a value S asserted and must be anchored (a truthiness spread would drop it).
388:       ...(data.identity?.actorDid !== undefined ? { actorDid: data.identity.actorDid } : {}),
389:       ...(data.identity?.actorLevel !== undefined ? { actorLevel: data.identity.actorLevel } : {}),
390:       ...(data.identity?.actorSource !== undefined ? { actorSource: data.identity.actorSource } : {}),
391:       ...(data.identity?.actorVerified !== undefined ? { actorVerified: data.identity.actorVerified } : {}),
392:       ...(data.identity?.orgDid !== undefined ? { orgDid: data.identity.orgDid } : {}),
393:       ...(data.identity?.orgTrust !== undefined ? { orgTrust: data.identity.orgTrust } : {}),
394:       ...(data.identity?.orgVerified !== undefined ? { orgVerified: data.identity.orgVerified } : {}),
395:     },
396:   };
397: }

### FILE AT HEAD: Blockchain/Dev/services/anchoring/src/anchorStatusUpdate.ts
1: /**
2:  * =============================================================================
3:  * KS-1385 MIN-1 — the anchor status UPDATE, built in one place
4:  * =============================================================================
5:  * WHY THIS MODULE EXISTS AT ALL. The invariant "the identity stored beside a
6:  * transaction hash is the identity of the event that produced that hash" does
7:  * not hold under concurrency once `metadata_payload` is writable. The pull
8:  * request 1549 gate measured it with the real `createAnchorSubmitter`, the real
9:  * wallet lock and real Postgres: with event 2's upsert landing while event 1 is
10:  * between "read the payload inside the lock" and "write submitted + tx1", the
11:  * row ends as `storedActor: did:key:zSHARER` beside
12:  * `actorInTheTxOnChain: did:key:zUPLOADER`, and `invariantHolds: false`.
13:  *
14:  * Wednesday ruled a compare-and-set in the submitter (2026-10-13T11:22:45Z).
15:  * The comparison has to be ATOMIC with the write, so it belongs in the
16:  * statement — and the statement was a template literal inlined in
17:  * `dbUpdateAnchorStatus`, where no cell can reach it.
18:  *
19:  * ⚠ AND THAT IS THE POINT OF THE EXTRACTION, not tidiness. The gate's race
20:  * cell — the one Wednesday told me to ADOPT as the red-first proof — hand-rolls
21:  * its own `updateStatus`, "mirroring index.ts dbUpdateAnchorStatus". A fix left
22:  * inside `dbUpdateAnchorStatus` would be proven by a cell that re-implements
23:  * `dbUpdateAnchorStatus`: the cell would pass against a product that still had
24:  * the defect. Extracted here, the cell imports the product's own builder and a
25:  * mutant on the compare-and-set reddens it.
26:  *
27:  * Pure: no database, no clock passed in by surprise, no environment. It returns
28:  * SQL text and bind values, so a cell can assert the shape AND execute it.
29:  * =============================================================================
30:  */
31: 
32: import { FABRICATED_TX_PREFIX } from './honestAnchor';
33: 
34: export interface AnchorStatusUpdate {
35:   status?: string;
36:   transactionHash?: string | null;
37:   blockHash?: string | null;
38:   blockNumber?: number | null;
39:   slot?: number | null;
40:   confirmations?: number | null;
41:   confirmedAt?: Date | null;
42:   errorMessage?: string | null;
43:   simulated?: boolean;
44:   /**
45:    * KS-1385 MIN-1 — the compare-and-set. When present, the UPDATE applies ONLY
46:    * if the row's `metadata_payload` is still this value: the payload the caller
47:    * read, signed and submitted. `metadata_payload` is `jsonb`, so the
48:    * comparison is semantic rather than textual and key order cannot make a
49:    * matching payload look different.
50:    *
51:    * A miss is not an error. It means a later event has already replaced the
52:    * payload, so this write would have recorded "event 2's identity, anchored by
53:    * event 1's transaction" — the falsification itself. Declining to write
54:    * leaves the row `pending` with no hash, which is exactly the state event 2's
55:    * own submission then resolves: it mints its own transaction and stores a
56:    * self-consistent row. Two genuinely different events produce two
57:    * transactions, which is honest; the double-mint guards (KS-705 / KS-726)
58:    * exist to stop the SAME payload being minted twice, and this is not that.
59:    */
60:   expectMetadataPayload?: object;
61:   /**
62:    * KS-1384 F-a — the row-count builder. When present, the UPDATE applies only
63:    * if the row's `status` is still this value (or one of these values). A
64:    * caller that read the row, did slow work (a chain check, a re-poll), and
65:    * then writes must not overwrite a row that moved meanwhile: the write
66:    * matches 0 rows instead, and the caller reads the 0 from
67:    * `runAnchorStatusUpdate`.
68:    */
69:   expectStatus?: string | string[];
70:   /**
71:    * KS-1384 F-a — the same, for `transaction_hash`. NULL-safe: `null` expects
72:    * a row with no hash (`IS NULL`), never `= NULL`, which matches nothing.
73:    */
74:   expectTransactionHash?: string | null;
75: }
76: 
77: export interface BuiltStatusUpdate {
78:   sql: string;
79:   params: unknown[];
80:   /** True when `expectMetadataPayload` was supplied, so a caller can tell a guarded write from an ordinary one. */
81:   guarded: boolean;
82:   /** KS-1384 F-a: true when any expectation (payload, status or hash) narrows the WHERE beyond the id. */
83:   conditional: boolean;
84: }
85: 
86: /**
87:  * Build the UPDATE for one anchor row. Returns null when there is nothing to
88:  * write — the caller must not issue a statement with an empty SET list.
89:  */
90: export function buildAnchorStatusUpdate(anchorId: string, updates: AnchorStatusUpdate, now: Date = new Date()): BuiltStatusUpdate | null {
91:   const setClauses: string[] = [];
92:   const params: unknown[] = [];
93:   let idx = 1;
94: 
95:   if (updates.status !== undefined) { setClauses.push(`status = $${idx++}`); params.push(updates.status); }
96:   if (updates.transactionHash !== undefined) {
97:     setClauses.push(`transaction_hash = $${idx++}`); params.push(updates.transactionHash);
98:     // KS-587: this UPDATE is how a processed anchor gains its hash — the row
99:     // was inserted pending (simulated=false, no hash) and this path is what
100:     // let mock_tx_ hashes land as simulated=false + confirmed. Recompute the
101:     // flag whenever the hash changes.
102:     setClauses.push(`simulated = $${idx++}`);
103:     params.push(
104:       updates.simulated === true ||
105:         FABRICATED_TX_PREFIX.test(updates.transactionHash || ''),
106:     );
107:   }
108:   if (updates.blockHash !== undefined) { setClauses.push(`block_hash = $${idx++}`); params.push(updates.blockHash); }
109:   if (updates.blockNumber !== undefined) { setClauses.push(`block_number = $${idx++}`); params.push(updates.blockNumber); }
110:   if (updates.slot !== undefined) { setClauses.push(`slot = $${idx++}`); params.push(updates.slot); }
111:   if (updates.confirmations !== undefined) { setClauses.push(`confirmations = $${idx++}`); params.push(updates.confirmations); }
112:   if (updates.confirmedAt !== undefined) { setClauses.push(`confirmed_at = $${idx++}`); params.push(updates.confirmedAt); }
113:   if (updates.errorMessage !== undefined) { setClauses.push(`error_message = $${idx++}`); params.push(updates.errorMessage); }
114: 
115:   if (setClauses.length === 0) return null;
116: 
117:   setClauses.push(`updated_at = $${idx++}`);
118:   params.push(now);
119: 
120:   const where: string[] = [`id = $${idx++}`];
121:   params.push(anchorId);
122: 
123:   const guarded = updates.expectMetadataPayload !== undefined;
124:   if (guarded) {
125:     where.push(`metadata_payload = $${idx++}::jsonb`);
126:     params.push(JSON.stringify(updates.expectMetadataPayload));
127:   }
128: 
129:   if (updates.expectStatus !== undefined) {
130:     if (Array.isArray(updates.expectStatus)) {
131:       where.push(`status = ANY($${idx++}::text[])`);
132:       params.push(updates.expectStatus);
133:     } else {
134:       where.push(`status = $${idx++}`);
135:       params.push(updates.expectStatus);
136:     }
137:   }
138: 
139:   if (updates.expectTransactionHash !== undefined) {
140:     if (updates.expectTransactionHash === null) {
141:       where.push('transaction_hash IS NULL');
142:     } else {
143:       where.push(`transaction_hash = $${idx++}`);
144:       params.push(updates.expectTransactionHash);
145:     }
146:   }
147: 
148:   return {
149:     sql: `UPDATE anchor_store SET ${setClauses.join(', ')} WHERE ${where.join(' AND ')}`,
150:     params,
151:     guarded,
152:     conditional: where.length > 1,
153:   };
154: }
155: 
156: export interface RunStatusUpdateDeps {
157:   isDbAvailable: () => boolean;
158:   ensureTables: () => Promise<unknown>;
159:   query: (sql: string, params?: unknown[]) => Promise<unknown>;
160:   log: (level: 'info' | 'warn' | 'error', message: string, meta?: Record<string, unknown>) => void;
161: }
162: 
163: /**
164:  * KS-1384 F-a — execute one status UPDATE and say how many rows it matched.
165:  *
166:  * This is the body `dbUpdateAnchorStatus` (index.ts) used to inline, moved here
167:  * so a cell can reach it without booting the service. The pin's version
168:  * returned nothing and swallowed every error, so a caller could not tell "the
169:  * row moved under me" (0 rows) from "the write never ran" (a database fault).
170:  *
171:  * Returns:
172:  *   - the matched row count (0 or 1 for an id-keyed UPDATE);
173:  *   - `null` when the write did not run or its result is unknown: the database
174:  *     is unavailable, there was nothing to write, the statement threw, or the
175:  *     driver reported no numeric `rowCount`.
176:  * A caller must treat `null` as "unknown", never as success. The errors are
177:  * still logged at warn and still not rethrown: every caller at this pin ignores
178:  * the result, and KS-1384 F0 is the first that reads it.
179:  */
180: export async function runAnchorStatusUpdate(
181:   deps: RunStatusUpdateDeps,
182:   anchorId: string,
183:   updates: AnchorStatusUpdate,
184:   now: Date = new Date(),
185: ): Promise<number | null> {
186:   try {
187:     if (!deps.isDbAvailable()) return null;
188:     await deps.ensureTables();
189: 
190:     const built = buildAnchorStatusUpdate(anchorId, updates, now);
191:     if (!built) return null;
192: 
193:     const result: any = await deps.query(built.sql, built.params);
194:     const affected = typeof result?.rowCount === 'number' ? result.rowCount : null;
195: 
196:     // KS-1385 MIN-1: a guarded write that matched NO row is not an error — it
197:     // is the race being caught. Say so once, at warn, because an operator
198:     // seeing a paid transaction with no row to show for it needs this line to
199:     // explain it. The transaction is on chain and honest; it just is not the
200:     // one this row indexes any more (KS-1384 is the structural ticket for a
201:     // row that can only index one).
202:     if (built.guarded && affected === 0) {
203:       deps.log('warn', 'KS-1385: declined to record a transaction against an anchor whose metadata changed under it — a later event had already replaced the payload', {
204:         anchorId,
205:         status: updates.status,
206:         transactionHash: updates.transactionHash,
207:       });
208:     }
209:     return affected;
210:   } catch (err: any) {
211:     deps.log('warn', 'Failed to update anchor status in DB', { anchorId, error: err?.message });
212:     return null;
213:   }
214: }

### FILE AT HEAD: Blockchain/Dev/services/anchoring/src/anchorSubmission.ts
1: /**
2:  * =============================================================================
3:  * KS-705 — ANCHOR SUBMISSION (idempotent on the SUBMISSION, not only the row)
4:  * =============================================================================
5:  * The real-Cardano half of `processAnchor`, extracted for the same
6:  * bootless-unit-test reason as `reconciler.ts` and `anchorSchema.ts`
7:  * (index.ts calls app.listen on import, and the only honest way to assert
8:  * "the submit path ran once" is against a fake chain — a real assertion costs
9:  * a real transaction).
10:  *
11:  * WHAT WENT WRONG (measured on demo 2026-08-27T23:54–23:55Z, KS-697 probe):
12:  * one document produced THREE `Submitting anchor to Cardano` calls for a
13:  * single anchor id and TWO confirmed, fee-paying transactions —
14:  * 408e7208… (block 4,609,927) and df797be3… (block 4,609,929), 181,825
15:  * lovelace each. `anchor_store` kept only the second. The first is on chain,
16:  * carrying the same metadata for the same document, and no row points at it.
17:  *
18:  * Two independent producers, both closed here:
19:  *
20:  *   1. A create + a custody transfer both call `POST /api/anchors` for one
21:  *      document. They correctly resolve to the SAME row (KS-319 — keep that),
22:  *      but both then entered the submit path. The second waited on the wallet
23:  *      lock and submitted a SECOND transaction for a document already
24:  *      anchored. A pre-lock read cannot see this: on demo the two calls were
25:  *      389 ms apart and the row was still 'pending' for both. The re-read
26:  *      INSIDE the lock is what makes the submission idempotent.
27:  *
28:  *   2. That second submission was rejected with
29:  *      `ConwayMempoolFailure "All inputs are spent. Transaction has probably
30:  *      already been included…"` — the node saying the opposite of failure. The
31:  *      catch marked the row 'failed' (clobbering a status that was already
32:  *      'confirmed'), reset it to 'pending' and submitted a THIRD transaction,
33:  *      which also confirmed.
34:  *
35:  * THE RULE: a reply that names an already-submitted transaction whose hash we
36:  * know is a re-poll condition, never a resubmit.
37:  *
38:  * NOT HANDLED — enumerated deliberately (KS-705); extend this list, not the
39:  * retry:
40:  *   - Any node reply other than the two texts matched below. Blockfrost
41:  *     surfaces a node rejection as an HTTP 400 whose `message` carries the
42:  *     ledger's prose; the SDK exposes no enumerated code for "already in
43:  *     ledger"/"duplicate tx", so there is nothing further to match that would
44:  *     be a read rather than a guess. Sibling replies known to exist but NOT
45:  *     observed by us, and so NOT matched: `TxValidationErrorInCardanoMode`
46:  *     wrappers, `BadInputsUTxO`, and any node-specific "transaction already
47:  *     exists"/"duplicate transaction" text.
48:  *   - "Already included" on a row carrying NO real transaction hash. We
49:  *     cannot re-poll a transaction we cannot name, so that case keeps today's
50:  *     fail-and-retry behaviour rather than being silently swallowed. This is
51:  *     also the KS-535 cross-deployment shape (two deployments on one wallet),
52:  *     which this module does not and cannot fix.
53:  *   - Two PROCESSES submitting for one anchor. `withWalletLock` and the
54:  *     in-lock re-read are both process-local. A second replica still races;
55:  *     that needs a DB-level claim and is not in scope here. KS-726 does not
56:  *     close this one either — a write-ahead is still a single process's
57:  *     record — so this entry stands as written.
58:  *
59:  * The "row write lost after a successful submit" window found on the #756
60:  * review is NOT in this list: guard 4 below closes it, by hoisting the hash
61:  * out of the lock callback so the catch can re-poll rather than retry.
62:  *
63:  * KS-726 (write-ahead) is now IMPLEMENTED here rather than pending: the hash is
64:  * persisted from inside `deps.submit` via `onSigned`, BEFORE the node is asked
65:  * to accept the transaction, so a LOST submit reply leaves a row that names the
66:  * transaction instead of one the retry path answers with a second fee-paying
67:  * transaction. That is the class guard 4 structurally cannot reach — guard 4
68:  * needs `deps.submit()` to have RETURNED. See guard 5 in the catch, the
69:  * 'submitting' state, and `knownChainSubmission` above. The two are
70:  * complementary, not alternatives.
71:  *
72:  * Guard 5 rests ONLY a LOST reply. A DEFINITIVE node rejection — the reply
73:  * ARRIVED and said no (`isChainReachableError`: a `status_code` on the error;
74:  * BadInputsUTxO, ValueNotConservedUTxO, an expired TTL, "All inputs are spent")
75:  * — keeps the ordinary fail-and-retry path: nothing is on chain and nothing was
76:  * paid, so a retry is safe and free, and resting such a row in 'submitting'
77:  * would trade a ~15 s recovery for the reconciler's 24 h give-up (#805 review,
78:  * 2026-09-08; ruled 2026-09-14). One consequence for guard 3, below: on an
79:  * "inputs spent" reply the hash the node just refused is on the row (the
80:  * write-ahead put it there) and IS re-polled first — that reply is also what
81:  * a node says when our own earlier submit already landed (KS-705) — and only
82:  * when the bounded poll shows it ABSENT does the ordinary retry follow.
83:  *
84:  * Still NOT closed by the write-ahead, and deliberately: a crash of the whole
85:  * process between signing and the 'submitting' row write. The write-ahead
86:  * shrinks that gap to a single DB round-trip but cannot remove it, because
87:  * something has to be the first durable record.
88:  * =============================================================================
89:  */
90: 
91: // KS-726 (#805 review, ruled 2026-09-14): the ONE thing this module imports.
92: // `isChainReachableError` is #728's discriminator — a `status_code` on the
93: // error means the node ANSWERED (any 4xx other than 402/408/429); no code, or
94: // 402/408/429/5xx, means it did not. Guard 5 below turns on it.
95: import { isChainReachableError } from './cardano/provider';
96: 
97: /** Only a 64-hex ref is a real chain transaction — `mock_tx_…`/`tx_sim_…` are not (KS-587). */
98: const REAL_TX_64HEX = /^[0-9a-f]{64}$/i;
99: 
100: /**
101:  * The node replies we have actually observed for "your inputs are gone because
102:  * your own earlier transaction is already in flight or in a block".
103:  *   - the first is the demo measurement (KS-705, 2026-08-27T23:55:12Z);
104:  *   - the second is the shorter form recorded against KS-535.
105:  * Anything else is left to the failure path on purpose — see NOT HANDLED above.
106:  */
107: /**
108:  * The one node reply that names THIS transaction as in the ledger. Guard 3
109:  * re-polls on it even when the hash on the row is the one just written ahead
110:  * (KS-726) — because there the node is saying our transaction landed.
111:  */
112: const ALREADY_INCLUDED_REPLY = /already been included/i;
113: 
114: const ALREADY_SUBMITTED_REPLIES = [
115:   /all inputs are spent/i,
116:   ALREADY_INCLUDED_REPLY,
117: ];
118: 
119: /** True when a submission error is the node telling us the tx is already in the ledger. */
120: export function isAlreadySubmittedError(err: unknown): boolean {
121:   const message = (err as { message?: unknown })?.message;
122:   if (typeof message !== 'string' || message.length === 0) return false;
123:   return ALREADY_SUBMITTED_REPLIES.some(pattern => pattern.test(message));
124: }
125: 
126: export interface AnchorSnapshot {
127:   id: string;
128:   status: 'pending' | 'submitting' | 'submitted' | 'confirmed' | 'failed';
129:   transactionHash?: string;
130:   retryCount: number;
131:   metadataLabel: number;
132:   metadataPayload: object;
133: }
134: 
135: export interface ConfirmationLike {
136:   confirmed: boolean;
137:   blockHash?: string;
138:   blockNumber?: number;
139:   slot?: number;
140:   confirmations?: number;
141:   /**
142:    * KS-726 (#805 gate F1): attempts that REACHED the chain / attempts that
143:    * THREW — `cardano/confirmation.ts` reports both. Optional so every injected
144:    * double stays type-compatible.
145:    *
146:    * ⚠ KS-1171 (Kam's ruling `c`, 2026-09-25) REVERSES what this comment used to
147:    * say. It read: "a `confirm` that reports neither keeps the pre-fix reading
148:    * (`confirmed: false` → 'absent')". That is no longer true and must not be
149:    * left standing: under the ruling a result carrying NO counters satisfies
150:    * neither ABSENT condition, so it now reads `'unknown'` and the row RESTS.
151:    * There is deliberately no carve-out for the counter-less shape — see the
152:    * gate in `reconfirmKnownSubmission`.
153:    */
154:   polled?: number;
155:   errored?: number;
156:   /**
157:    * KS-1171: WHEN the chain last answered. `lastAnsweredElapsedMs` is measured
158:    * from the START OF POLLING, so a threshold on it is strictly conservative
159:    * against "60 s after the node's 400" — polling begins at or after that 400.
160:    */
161:   lastAnsweredAttempt?: number;
162:   lastAnsweredElapsedMs?: number;
163: }
164: 
165: /**
166:  * KS-1171, Kam's ruling (c). The two conditions a poll must BOTH meet before its verdict may be
167:  * read as a definitive "this transaction is not on chain". Exported so a regression cell names the
168:  * rule rather than repeating its numbers.
169:  */
170: export const ABSENT_MIN_ANSWERED_POLLS = 2;
171: export const ABSENT_MIN_LAST_ANSWER_MS = 60_000;
172: 
173: export interface AnchorSubmissionDeps {
174:   getAnchor: (anchorId: string) => Promise<AnchorSnapshot | null>;
175:   /**
176:    * KS-726: the optional third argument carries the write-ahead hook. Optional
177:    * so every existing injector — and #756's own two-argument call — stays
178:    * type-compatible; `cardano/index.ts`'s `AnchorSubmitOptions` is already
179:    * shaped for it.
180:    */
181:   submit: (
182:     metadataLabel: number,
183:     metadataPayload: object,
184:     options?: { onSigned?: (txHash: string) => Promise<void> },
185:   ) => Promise<{ txHash: string; fee: number }>;
186:   confirm: (txHash: string, onStatusUpdate?: (attempt: number, status: string) => void) => Promise<ConfirmationLike>;
187:   /**
188:    * KS-1384 F-a: the production write (`dbUpdateAnchorStatus`) now resolves to the matched row count, or
189:    * null when the write did not run. This submitter does not read it yet (KS-1384 F0 is the first reader);
190:    * `void` stays in the union so every existing injector remains type-compatible until then.
191:    */
192:   updateStatus: (anchorId: string, updates: Record<string, unknown>) => Promise<number | null | void>;
193:   bumpRetryCount: (anchorId: string) => Promise<void>;
194:   scheduleRetry: (anchorId: string, delayMs: number) => void;
195:   log: (level: 'info' | 'warn' | 'error', message: string, meta?: Record<string, unknown>) => void;
196:   /** The shared process-wide wallet mutex — see `withWalletLock` below. */
197:   withWalletLock?: <T>(fn: () => Promise<T>) => Promise<T>;
198:   maxRetries?: number;
199:   backoffMsPerAttempt?: number;
200: }
201: 
202: /**
203:  * Serialize Cardano submissions against the platform wallet. The UTXO model
204:  * means two concurrent submissions race to spend the same UTxO; the second one
205:  * fails with "All inputs are spent". We take a Promise chain as a process-local
206:  * mutex so each submission only begins once the prior one has committed. This
207:  * is the minimum viable solution — at scale we'd batch or use multi-UTxO coin
208:  * selection.
209:  *
210:  * Exported so the batch-anchor path in index.ts shares the SAME chain: two
211:  * independent locks on one wallet would be no lock at all.
212:  */
213: let walletSubmissionChain: Promise<unknown> = Promise.resolve();
214: export function withWalletLock<T>(fn: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {
215:   const next = walletSubmissionChain.catch(() => undefined).then(() => fn());
216:   walletSubmissionChain = next.catch(() => undefined);
217:   return next;
218: }
219: 
220: /**
221:  * The hash of an existing REAL chain submission for this anchor, if there is one.
222:  *
223:  * KS-726: 'submitting' counts. It is the write-ahead state — the row names a
224:  * transaction that was SIGNED, and whose submit reply we never received, so a
225:  * transaction bearing our name may already be on chain and fee-paying.
226:  *
227:  * Leaving it out is a reachable double-mint that the write-ahead itself opens,
228:  * not a hypothetical. `dbPersistAnchor` returns the EXISTING row's id on a
229:  * re-anchor of the same document+network (KS-281/KS-319) and `POST /api/anchors`
230:  * then calls `processAnchor` unconditionally (index.ts). So a second create — or
231:  * the custody-transfer hop that KS-705's own header describes — lands on a row
232:  * resting in 'submitting', both guards below see "no known submission", and the
233:  * path mints a SECOND transaction for a document whose first one may already be
234:  * in a block. That is the exact defect this module exists to prevent, reached
235:  * through the front door rather than the retry.
236:  *
237:  * Before KS-726 the case could not arise: a lost reply wrote 'failed' and
238:  * resolved to 'pending', and the retry path owned it. The write-ahead is what
239:  * makes the row REST in a state the guards could not read — so the guard has to
240:  * learn the state its own change creates.
241:  *
242:  * Re-polling such a row is safe and is the weaker-harm choice, on the same
243:  * asymmetry guard 5 turns on: `reconfirmKnownSubmission` is bounded, leaves a
244:  * confirmed row alone, and NEVER writes 'failed' — worst case it times out,
245:  * logs, and leaves the row for the reconciler, which owns the give-up decision
246:  * (KS-584). A delayed anchor is recoverable; a duplicate fee-paying transaction
247:  * is not.
248:  */
249: function knownChainSubmission(anchor: AnchorSnapshot | null): string | undefined {
250:   if (!anchor) return undefined;
251:   if (anchor.status !== 'submitting' && anchor.status !== 'submitted' && anchor.status !== 'confirmed') {
252:     return undefined;
253:   }
254:   const tx = anchor.transactionHash ?? '';
255:   return REAL_TX_64HEX.test(tx) ? tx : undefined;
256: }
257: 
258: export function createAnchorSubmitter(deps: AnchorSubmissionDeps) {
259:   const lock = deps.withWalletLock ?? withWalletLock;
260:   const maxRetries = deps.maxRetries ?? 3;
261:   const backoffMsPerAttempt = deps.backoffMsPerAttempt ?? 5000;
262: 
263:   /**
264:    * Bring the row into line with a transaction that is ALREADY on its way —
265:    * never a new submission. Leaves a confirmed row alone; never writes 'failed'
266:    * (a tx we have not found yet is not a tx that failed — the reconciler owns
267:    * the give-up decision, KS-584).
268:    */
269:   async function reconfirmKnownSubmission(
270:     anchorId: string,
271:     txHash: string,
272:     why: string,
273:   ): Promise<'confirmed' | 'absent' | 'unknown'> {
274:     // The return value is read by guard 3 only (KS-726): 'absent' means the
275:     // bounded poll ENDED without finding the transaction — the chain answered
276:     // and it is not there; 'unknown' means the poll itself failed — it threw,
277:     // or no attempt ever reached the chain — which says nothing about the
278:     // chain and keeps today's behaviour.
279:     deps.log('info', 'Anchor already submitted — re-polling the known transaction instead of submitting again (KS-705)', {
280:       anchorId, txHash, why,
281:     });
282:     const current = await deps.getAnchor(anchorId);
283:     if (current?.status === 'confirmed') return 'confirmed';
284:     try {
285:       const confirmation = await deps.confirm(txHash, (attempt, status) =>
286:         deps.log('info', 'Anchor confirmation poll', { anchorId, attempt, status }));
287:       if (confirmation.confirmed) {
288:         await deps.updateStatus(anchorId, {
289:           status: 'confirmed',
290:           // KS-705 (#756 review): name the transaction that confirmed it.
291:           // Without this we confirm a row while never recording WHICH
292:           // transaction confirmed it — the same orphan this module exists to
293:           // prevent, one step later. The re-poll path is reached from rows
294:           // that already carry the hash AND from the catch below, where the
295:           // row write may never have landed.
296:           transactionHash: txHash,
297:           confirmations: confirmation.confirmations ?? 1,
298:           blockNumber: confirmation.blockNumber,
299:           slot: confirmation.slot,
300:           blockHash: confirmation.blockHash,
301:           confirmedAt: new Date(),
302:         });
303:         deps.log('info', 'Anchor confirmed on Cardano', {
304:           anchorId, txHash, blockNumber: confirmation.blockNumber, slot: confirmation.slot,
305:         });
306:         return 'confirmed';
307:       }
308:       // #805 tier-1 gate F1 (2026-09-14): the poll ended without ONE attempt
309:       // reaching the chain — every attempt threw (Blockfrost 5xx / 402 quota,
310:       // DNS, socket). That is not "the chain answered and it is not there"; it
311:       // is "we never asked". Reporting 'absent' here let guard 3 read an
312:       // unreachable chain as a DEFINITIVE rejection and schedule the ordinary
313:       // retry, which mints a SECOND fee-paying transaction for a document
314:       // whose first may already be in a block — the KS-705 class, reached
315:       // through the write-ahead path. 'unknown' rests the row in 'submitting'
316:       // for the reconciler (KS-584): the same weaker-harm choice guard 5 makes
317:       // for a lost reply. A `confirm` that reports no `polled` count (an
318:       // injected double) keeps the 'absent' reading.
319:       // KS-1171 — RULED BY KAM, option (c), live board 2026-09-25 12:49:07 AEST, card
320:       // `secuura-ks1171-when-is-an-anchor-absent`, verbatim: "c - Both conditions (strictest,
321:       // fewest double submissions)". A transaction may be called ABSENT — and so retried, which
322:       // mints a SECOND fee-paying transaction — only when BOTH hold:
323:       //
324:       //   1. at least 2 attempts ANSWERED (the chain replied, with the tx or with "not found"), and
325:       //   2. an answer arrived at least 60 s after the node's 400.
326:       //
327:       // Anything less rests the row in 'submitting' for the reconciler (KS-584) as 'unknown'. KS-726
328:       // closed the case where NO attempt reached the chain; this closes its neighbour, where one
329:       // early "not found" was read as definitive. Blockfrost answers 404 until a transaction is in a
330:       // block AND indexed, so an early answer is a likely FALSE negative in exactly the case that
331:       // matters — a same-hash duplicate submit, where TX_A entered the mempool seconds before the 400.
332:       //
333:       // The timing is measured from the START OF POLLING, which is at or after the 400. So
334:       // `lastAnsweredElapsedMs >= 60_000` IMPLIES at least 60 s since the 400, never less: the gate
335:       // is strictly conservative against the ruling, which is the side Kam chose.
336:       //
337:       // The cost, accepted with the ruling: a genuinely absent transaction whose late attempts threw
338:       // now rests ~24 h for the reconciler instead of retrying in ~15 s.
339:       //
340:       // A `confirm` that reports NO counters (an injected double) satisfies neither condition and so
341:       // reads 'unknown'. That REVERSES the KS-726 note and is deliberate — see
342:       // `ks726-gate-f1-unreachable-chain.test.ts`, whose control is rewritten to assert it. There is
343:       // no carve-out for `polled === undefined`: a branch whose only consumer is a test double later
344:       // reads as intended behaviour, and production always goes through `waitForConfirmation`, which
345:       // always reports both counters.
346:       const answered = confirmation.polled ?? 0;
347:       const lastAnsweredElapsedMs = confirmation.lastAnsweredElapsedMs;
348:       const answeredTwice = answered >= ABSENT_MIN_ANSWERED_POLLS;
349:       const answeredLateEnough =
350:         typeof lastAnsweredElapsedMs === 'number' && lastAnsweredElapsedMs >= ABSENT_MIN_LAST_ANSWER_MS;
351:       if (!answeredTwice || !answeredLateEnough) {
352:         // Two DIFFERENT operational facts, so two different messages. KS-726's case — no attempt
353:         // reached the chain at all — keeps its own wording, because "never reached the chain" is
354:         // still exactly what happened and an operator reading the log needs to tell it from "the
355:         // chain answered, but not definitively enough to retry on". Collapsing them into one
356:         // message also silently broke three shipped KS-726 cells that assert the KS-726 text while
357:         // asserting UNCHANGED behaviour; that was a defect in this change, not a consequence of
358:         // the ruling.
359:         const neverReached = answered === 0;
360:         deps.log(
361:           'warn',
362:           neverReached
363:             ? 'Anchor confirmation poll never reached the chain — treating the transaction as UNKNOWN, not absent (KS-726)'
364:             : 'Anchor confirmation answered but is not definitive — treating the transaction as UNKNOWN, not absent (KS-1171)',
365:           {
366:             anchorId, txHash,
367:             polled: confirmation.polled, errored: confirmation.errored,
368:             lastAnsweredAttempt: confirmation.lastAnsweredAttempt,
369:             lastAnsweredElapsedMs,
370:             answeredTwice, answeredLateEnough,
371:           },
372:         );
373:         return 'unknown';
374:       }
375:       deps.log('warn', 'Anchor confirmation timed out', { anchorId, txHash, polled: confirmation.polled, errored: confirmation.errored, lastAnsweredElapsedMs });
376:       return 'absent';
377:     } catch (err) {
378:       deps.log('error', 'Anchor confirmation polling failed', { anchorId, error: (err as Error)?.message });
379:       return 'unknown';
380:     }
381:   }
382: 
383:   /** Submit one anchor to Cardano — at most one transaction per anchor. */
384:   return async function submitAnchorToChain(anchorId: string): Promise<void> {
385:     const anchor = await deps.getAnchor(anchorId);
386:     if (!anchor) return;
387: 
388:     // Guard 1 (cheap, pre-lock): this anchor already has a transaction on chain.
389:     const alreadyKnown = knownChainSubmission(anchor);
390:     if (alreadyKnown) {
391:       await reconfirmKnownSubmission(anchorId, alreadyKnown, 'row already carries a chain submission');
392:       return;
393:     }
394: 
395:     // KS-705 (#756 review): the hash of a transaction we have already put on
396:     // chain, hoisted out of the lock callback so the catch below can see it.
397:     //
398:     // `deps.submit()` mints and pays for a transaction; the row write on the
399:     // NEXT line records its hash. If that write throws — a Postgres blip, pool
400:     // exhaustion, a restart — the hash dies with the stack frame, the catch
401:     // sees an ordinary error, and the retry submits a SECOND transaction for a
402:     // document that is already anchored. Guards 1 and 2 cannot see it: they
403:     // read the ROW, and the row is exactly what failed to be written.
404:     let submittedTxHash: string | undefined;
405: 
406:     // KS-726: the write-ahead hash — set from inside `deps.submit` via
407:     // `onSigned`, BEFORE the node is asked to accept the transaction.
408:     //
409:     // Declared OUT here for exactly the reason `submittedTxHash` is: a variable
410:     // the catch cannot see is precisely the defect this closes. #764 first wrote
411:     // it inside the try and `tsc` rejected the catch with TS2304 — the compiler
412:     // reproducing the ticket in miniature.
413:     //
414:     // The two are NOT the same fact and they do not have the same remedy:
415:     //   - `submittedTxHash` set  ⇒ `deps.submit()` RETURNED ⇒ the node
416:     //     acknowledged. Re-polling is safe (guard 4).
417:     //   - `writtenAheadTxHash` set and `submittedTxHash` NOT ⇒ the transaction
418:     //     was signed and named, and the reply was LOST. Acceptance is unknown.
419:     let writtenAheadTxHash: string | undefined;
420: 
421:     try {
422:       // Hold the wallet lock through submit + confirmation. The Cardano UTXO
423:       // model means a freshly-spent UTxO stays in Blockfrost's "available"
424:       // view until the tx is in a block (~20–60s). Concurrent submissions race
425:       // and the second one fails with "All inputs are spent". This design
426:       // serializes at the wallet level: at most one anchor tx in-flight from
427:       // the platform wallet. Throughput is intentionally bounded (~one cert
428:       // per block slot). A proper multi-UTXO coin selector is the
429:       // commercialisation upgrade path.
430:       const outcome = await lock(async (): Promise<{ kind: 'submitted' } | { kind: 'already'; txHash: string }> => {
431:         // Guard 2 (KS-705): re-read INSIDE the lock. Guard 1 ran before we
432:         // queued; by the time we hold the lock, the call ahead of us may have
433:         // submitted this very anchor. This is the guard that would have stopped
434:         // demo's second transaction.
435:         const current = (await deps.getAnchor(anchorId)) ?? anchor;
436:         const known = knownChainSubmission(current);
437:         if (known) return { kind: 'already', txHash: known };
438: 
439:         // KS-1385 MIN-1 — the compare-and-set value. This is the payload we are
440:         // about to put ON CHAIN, so it is the only payload a transaction hash
441:         // written below may legitimately be recorded beside. Every write in
442:         // this callback that CLAIMS a transaction carries it as
443:         // `expectMetadataPayload`, and the UPDATE applies only while the row
444:         // still holds it (anchorStatusUpdate.ts).
445:         //
446:         // Ruled by Wednesday 2026-10-13T11:22:45Z after the gate measured the
447:         // window with the real lock and real Postgres: without this, a second
448:         // event's upsert landing mid-submit leaves the row carrying event 2's
449:         // identity next to event 1's transaction.
450:         //
451:         // Captured HERE, not at the top of `submitAnchorToChain`: the read
452:         // outside the lock can be arbitrarily stale, and the fact that matters
453:         // is what we signed, which is what `current` holds now.
454:         const signedMetadataPayload = current.metadataPayload;
455: 
456:         deps.log('info', 'Submitting anchor to Cardano', { anchorId });
457: 
458:         const submission = await deps.submit(current.metadataLabel, current.metadataPayload, {
459:           // KS-726 — write-ahead. Runs after the transaction is built and
460:           // signed, before it is handed to the network. The hash is the
461:           // blake2b-256 of the signed body, so it is fully known here; nothing
462:           // the network says afterwards can teach us anything about it.
463:           onSigned: async (txHash) => {
464:             // #805 review F3: assigned AFTER the write, NOT before — the mirror
465:             // of `submittedTxHash` below, and the inverse case.
466:             //
467:             // `submittedTxHash` is captured before its write because by then the
468:             // transaction HAS been submitted: the fact must survive a failing
469:             // row write. Here the opposite holds — `onSigned` runs BEFORE the
470:             // network is asked, so if this write throws, NO transaction exists.
471:             // Assigning first would arm guard 5, which rests the row in
472:             // 'submitting' and schedules no retry, for a transaction that was
473:             // never sent — where an ordinary retry is correct and free.
474:             //
475:             // Assigned here, the variable means what guard 5 reads it as: the
476:             // hash is durable. Unreachable today only because the injected
477:             // updateStatus swallows (see the contract note in cardano/index.ts);
478:             // one refactor away from being real, and guard 4's own test already
479:             // simulates this shape.
480:             await deps.updateStatus(anchorId, { status: 'submitting', transactionHash: txHash, expectMetadataPayload: signedMetadataPayload });
481:             writtenAheadTxHash = txHash;
482:           },
483:         });
484:         // Assign BEFORE the row write, not after: the write is the thing that
485:         // can fail, and this assignment is what survives it.
486:         submittedTxHash = submission.txHash;
487:         await deps.updateStatus(anchorId, { status: 'submitted', transactionHash: submission.txHash, expectMetadataPayload: signedMetadataPayload });
488:         deps.log('info', 'Anchor submitted to Cardano', { anchorId, txHash: submission.txHash, fee: submission.fee });
489: 
490:         // Block inside the lock until confirmed (or timed out). The next anchor
491:         // waiting on the lock will then read UTxOs that reflect this tx.
492:         try {
493:           const confirmation = await deps.confirm(submission.txHash, (attempt, status) =>
494:             deps.log('info', 'Anchor confirmation poll', { anchorId, attempt, status }));
495:           if (confirmation.confirmed) {
496:             await deps.updateStatus(anchorId, {
497:               status: 'confirmed',
498:               confirmations: confirmation.confirmations ?? 1,
499:               blockNumber: confirmation.blockNumber,
500:               slot: confirmation.slot,
501:               blockHash: confirmation.blockHash,
502:               confirmedAt: new Date(),
503:               // KS-1385 MIN-1: guarded for the same reason as the two writes
504:               // above, and NOT as an afterthought. Without it, a row whose
505:               // 'submitted' write was correctly declined would still be marked
506:               // `confirmed` — status confirmed, transaction_hash null — which
507:               // is a worse row than the one the guard refused to write.
508:               expectMetadataPayload: signedMetadataPayload,
509:             });
510:             deps.log('info', 'Anchor confirmed on Cardano', {
511:               anchorId,
512:               txHash: submission.txHash,
513:               blockNumber: confirmation.blockNumber,
514:               slot: confirmation.slot,
515:             });
516:           } else {
517:             deps.log('warn', 'Anchor confirmation timed out', { anchorId, txHash: submission.txHash });
518:           }
519:         } catch (err) {
520:           deps.log('error', 'Anchor confirmation polling failed', { anchorId, error: (err as Error).message });
521:         }
522:         return { kind: 'submitted' };
523:       });
524: 
525:       // Re-polling happens OUTSIDE the lock: we are not spending a UTxO, so
526:       // holding the wallet hostage for another anchor's confirmation window
527:       // would throttle the platform for no benefit.
528:       if (outcome.kind === 'already') {
529:         await reconfirmKnownSubmission(anchorId, outcome.txHash, 'another call submitted this anchor while we waited on the wallet lock');
530:       }
531:     } catch (err: unknown) {
532:       const message = (err as { message?: string })?.message;
533: 
534:       // Guard 4 (KS-705, #756 review): we know we put a transaction on chain,
535:       // whatever this error says. Checked FIRST, before the node-reply matcher
536:       // and before any failure is recorded, because it is the strongest fact
537:       // available here: a hash in this variable means `deps.submit()` returned,
538:       // so the transaction is real and fee-paying. Answering that with another
539:       // transaction is the double-mint; marking the row terminally 'failed'
540:       // would propagate a permanent document failure for an anchor that
541:       // succeeded. Re-poll instead — the same treatment as the node's
542:       // "already included" reply, reached by a different route.
543:       if (submittedTxHash && REAL_TX_64HEX.test(submittedTxHash)) {
544:         deps.log('warn', 'Anchor submission threw AFTER the transaction reached the chain — re-polling the known hash instead of retrying (KS-705)', {
545:           anchorId, txHash: submittedTxHash, error: message,
546:         });
547:         await reconfirmKnownSubmission(anchorId, submittedTxHash, 'the row write failed after a successful submit');
548:         return;
549:       }
550: 
551:       // Guard 5 (KS-726) — the write-ahead branch. Sits BELOW guard 4 and
552:       // ABOVE guard 3 and the 'failed' write, because the catch is ordered by
553:       // the STRENGTH OF THE FACT each branch rests on, strongest first:
554:       //
555:       //   1. guard 4  — `deps.submit()` returned          → the node acknowledged
556:       //   2. guard 5  — signed and named, reply LOST      → acceptance UNKNOWN
557:       //   3. guard 3  — an inferred error-message shape   → weakest
558:       //   4. fall through — nothing
559:       //
560:       // Guard 5 covers ONE case: the reply was LOST. It does NOT cover a
561:       // DEFINITIVE rejection — the reply ARRIVED and said no. After the
562:       // write-ahead `writtenAheadTxHash` is set for EVERY real submission, so
563:       // without the discriminator below this branch would catch every error
564:       // thrown after signing and the retry path would be unreachable for the
565:       // whole class (#805 review, 2026-09-08). `isChainReachableError` (#728) is
566:       // that discriminator: a `status_code` on the error means the node
567:       // answered. A refused transaction is definitively NOT on chain and
568:       // nothing was paid, so it falls through to guard 3 and the ordinary
569:       // fail-and-retry write — a ~15 s recovery instead of the reconciler's
570:       // 24 h give-up on the most ordinary Cardano failure class. Ruled
571:       // 2026-09-14 (option 1 of the two the review offered).
572:       //
573:       // Why the LOST-reply case does NOT re-poll: after a lost reply we do not know the node
574:       // accepted it. Polling could block on a transaction that never landed;
575:       // writing 'failed' could permanently fail a document whose transaction is
576:       // on chain and fee-paying. The harms are asymmetric, so the guard resolves
577:       // toward the RECOVERABLE state — 'submitting', which the reconciler
578:       // selects (reconciler.ts) and settles either way.
579:       //
580:       // Its position is load-bearing, not cosmetic. #764's own review found the
581:       // 'failed' write landing FIRST, with the write-ahead check ~25 lines
582:       // later. Pre-KS-726 that flash resolved back to 'pending' and the row
583:       // re-entered the normal path — the KS-535 trade-off. WITH a write-ahead it
584:       // resolved to 'submitting' and STAYED, so the row spent a real window
585:       // claiming 'failed'. 'failed' is terminal by construction (KS-535) and
586:       // originate's poller acts on it, so a poller ticking inside that window
587:       // could propagate a PERMANENT document failure for a transaction that is
588:       // on chain. Ordering it here removes the window rather than documenting it.
589:       if (writtenAheadTxHash && REAL_TX_64HEX.test(writtenAheadTxHash) && isChainReachableError(err)) {
590:         // The node ANSWERED and refused the transaction we signed. Nothing is
591:         // on chain, nothing was paid: fall through to guard 3 and the terminal
592:         // 'failed' write + retry below, exactly as a pre-write-ahead failure did.
593:         deps.log('warn', 'Node REFUSED the written-ahead transaction — the reply arrived, nothing is on chain, keeping the ordinary fail-and-retry path (KS-726)', {
594:           anchorId, txHash: writtenAheadTxHash, statusCode: (err as { status_code?: number })?.status_code, error: message,
595:         });
596:       } else if (writtenAheadTxHash && REAL_TX_64HEX.test(writtenAheadTxHash)) {
597:         await deps.updateStatus(anchorId, {
598:           status: 'submitting',
599:           // #805 review F2: carry the hash. The injected updateStatus SWALLOWS
600:           // its errors, so a DB blip during the write-ahead does not throw and
601:           // the submit proceeds anyway. If the reply is then lost, this guard
602:           // fires for a row that names no transaction — invisible to the
603:           // reconciler (`transaction_hash IS NOT NULL`), invisible to
604:           // knownChainSubmission (the empty fallback fails REAL_TX_64HEX), and
605:           // never retried, because this branch returns without scheduleRetry.
606:           // A stranded row with a paid transaction on chain: the exact failure
607:           // this ticket exists to close, surviving in a narrower window.
608:           // Idempotent when the write-ahead landed; the whole fix when it did not.
609:           transactionHash: writtenAheadTxHash,
610:           errorMessage: `Submit reply lost after signing; tx ${writtenAheadTxHash} may be on chain — left for the reconciler (KS-726)`,
611:         });
612:         // #805 review (2026-09-08), note 2: NO `bumpRetryCount` here. No retry is
613:         // coming from this branch, the reconciler's give-up is time-based
614:         // (`giveUpMs` against `created_at`, reconciler.ts), so a bump would only
615:         // spend budget that a later front-door re-anchor inherits reduced.
616:         deps.log('warn', 'Anchor submit failed AFTER the hash was written ahead — not retrying, reconciler will settle it', {
617:           anchorId, txHash: writtenAheadTxHash, error: message,
618:         });
619:         return;
620:       }
621: 
622:       // Guard 3 (KS-705): the node telling us the inputs are spent because our
623:       // own transaction is already in the ledger is not a failure. Re-poll the
624:       // hash we know; never answer it with another transaction.
625:       if (isAlreadySubmittedError(err)) {
626:         const current = await deps.getAnchor(anchorId);
627:         const known = knownChainSubmission(current);
628:         if (known) {
629:           const found = await reconfirmKnownSubmission(anchorId, known, 'node reported the transaction is already included');
630:           // KS-726 (ruled 2026-09-14): the write-ahead put the hash of the
631:           // transaction we JUST signed on the row before the node answered, so
632:           // `known` can be that very hash. "inputs spent" is what a node says
633:           // BOTH when our own earlier submit already landed (KS-705 — the
634:           // re-poll above is the right answer, and skipping it is the
635:           // double-mint) AND when someone else spent our inputs (KS-535 — our
636:           // transaction can never land). The reply text cannot tell the two
637:           // apart; the chain can. So the re-poll runs first, bounded, and only
638:           // when it shows the hash we just wrote ahead ABSENT is the rejection
639:           // definitive — then the ordinary retry below follows, instead of the
640:           // reconciler's 24 h give-up. "already been included" names THIS
641:           // transaction as in the ledger and never reaches this branch's retry.
642:           const refusedAndAbsent =
643:             found === 'absent' &&
644:             known === writtenAheadTxHash &&
645:             !ALREADY_INCLUDED_REPLY.test(message ?? '');
646:           if (!refusedAndAbsent) return;
647:           deps.log('warn', 'The transaction just written ahead is ABSENT on chain after the re-poll and the node said "inputs spent" — a definitive rejection; keeping the ordinary fail-and-retry path (KS-726)', {
648:             anchorId, txHash: known, error: message,
649:           });
650:         } else {
651:           // No hash of ours to re-poll — fall through. Swallowing this would
652:           // strand the anchor with no record and no retry (see NOT HANDLED).
653:           deps.log('warn', 'Node reported an already-included transaction, but this anchor has no known tx hash to re-poll (KS-705)', {
654:             anchorId, error: message,
655:           });
656:         }
657:       }
658: 
659:       const currentRetry = anchor.retryCount + 1;
660: 
661:       await deps.updateStatus(anchorId, {
662:         status: 'failed',
663:         errorMessage: message || 'Cardano submission failed',
664:       });
665:       await deps.bumpRetryCount(anchorId);
666: 
667:       deps.log('error', 'Anchor submission failed', { anchorId, error: message, retryCount: currentRetry });
668: 
669:       // Retry up to `maxRetries` times with backoff.
670:       // KS-535: reset to 'pending' BEFORE the backoff timer starts, not inside
671:       // it — this makes `status: 'failed'` TERMINAL (no retry coming), which
672:       // originate's poller/reconciler relies on to propagate a failure to the
673:       // document. Previously the row sat as 'failed' for the 5–10s backoff
674:       // window, so an observer could not distinguish "retrying" from "given
675:       // up". (Trade-off: a crash during the backoff now leaves the row
676:       // 'pending' instead of 'failed' — a stuck-pending, same as any other
677:       // restart-lost anchor; anchoring has no restart re-queue either way.)
678:       if (currentRetry < maxRetries) {
679:         const backoff = currentRetry * backoffMsPerAttempt;
680:         deps.log('info', `Scheduling retry in ${backoff}ms`, { anchorId, retryCount: currentRetry });
681:         await deps.updateStatus(anchorId, { status: 'pending' });
682:         deps.scheduleRetry(anchorId, backoff);
683:       }
684:     }
685:   };
686: }

### FILE AT HEAD: Blockchain/Dev/services/anchoring/src/anchoring.openapi.ts
1: /**
2:  * =============================================================================
3:  * ANCHORING SERVICE — OpenAPI registrations (Track 2 proof-of-concept)
4:  * =============================================================================
5:  *
6:  * Worked example for the Zod-to-OpenAPI migration recipe in
7:  * `TRACK2_OPENAPI_MIGRATION.md`. Walks the anchoring service's primary
8:  * routes + state-thread NFT endpoints (today's F6 Phase 2 work) and
9:  * registers them with the shared registry.
10:  *
11:  * The generator script (`scripts/generate-openapi.mjs`) imports this
12:  * file, the registry gets populated as a side-effect of import, then
13:  * the document is generated from `sharedRegistry`.
14:  *
15:  * Migration recipe for the next service:
16:  *   1. Copy this file's structure to `services/<svc>/src/<svc>.openapi.ts`
17:  *   2. Import the service's existing Zod request/response schemas
18:  *      (from packages/shared/src/schemas/* or local definitions)
19:  *   3. Call `sharedRegistry.registerPath({...})` for each route
20:  *   4. Run `npm run generate-openapi` to regenerate the YAML
21:  *   5. Verify the new endpoints render in Swagger UI at /api/docs
22:  * =============================================================================
23:  */
24:

260:         identityCommitment: z.string().max(128).regex(/^[a-z0-9-]+\.v\d+:[0-9a-f]{64}$/).optional().openapi({

262:             'KS-721: Platform-S\'s opaque keyed identity commitment, `<label>.v<N>:<64 lowercase hex>` ' +

87: // AUDIT B-16: allowlisted set of Cardano networks. Any request that asks
88: // for a network outside this set is rejected; cross-network confusion
89: // (mainnet tx replayed on preprod application-side, etc.) is blocked at

100: /** AUDIT B-16: validate a per-request network parameter against the allowlist. */

102:   return typeof n === 'string' && ALLOWED_CARDANO_NETWORKS.has(n);
