105: // =============================================================================
106: // TYPE DEFINITIONS
107: // =============================================================================
108: 
109: interface Anchor {
110:   id: string;
111:   documentId: string;
112:   anchorType: 'single' | 'batch';
113:   contentHash: string;
114:   merkleRoot?: string;
115:   merkleProof?: MerkleProofStep[];
116:   transactionHash?: string;
117:   blockHash?: string;
118:   blockNumber?: number;
119:   slot?: number;
120:   network: string;
121:   metadataLabel: number;
122:   metadataPayload: object;
123:   /**
124:    * KS-726 adds 'submitting': the transaction is signed and its deterministic
125:    * hash is persisted, but the node has not yet acknowledged it. Distinct from
126:    * 'submitted' (node accepted) and from 'pending' (nothing signed yet), because
127:    * only this state means "a transaction bearing our name may already be on
128:    * chain". 'failed' remains TERMINAL by construction (KS-535) — originate's
129:    * poller depends on that and this state does not disturb it.
130:    */
131:   status: 'pending' | 'submitting' | 'submitted' | 'confirmed' | 'failed';
132:   fee?: number;
133:   confirmations: number;
134:   confirmedAt?: Date;
135:   errorMessage?: string;
136:   retryCount: number;
137:   // KS-587: true when this anchor was produced by the mock/simulated provider
138:   // and corresponds to no Cardano transaction.
139:   simulated?: boolean;
140:   createdAt: Date;
141:   updatedAt: Date;
142: }
143: 
144: interface MerkleProofStep {
145:   hash: string;
146:   position: 'left' | 'right';
147: }
148: 
149: interface MerkleBatch {
150:   id: string;
151:   documentIds: string[];
152:   merkleRoot: string;
153:   documentCount: number;
154:   status: 'pending' | 'submitted' | 'confirmed' | 'failed';
155:   anchorId?: string;
156:   createdAt: Date;
157: }
158: 
159: // =============================================================================
160: // STORAGE: All data is persisted in PostgreSQL (anchor_store, anchor_batches)
161: // No in-memory Maps — every read/write goes through the DB helpers below.
162: // =============================================================================
163: 
164: // =============================================================================
165: // DATABASE TABLES (ensured on first write)
166: // =============================================================================
167: 
168: let tablesEnsured = false;
169: 
170: async function ensureTables(): Promise<void> {
171:   if (tablesEnsured || !isDbAvailable()) return;
172:   try {
173:     // KS-92: anchor_store + anchor_batches are owned by the superuser migration
174:     // path (api-gateway CORE_MIGRATIONS). The runtime role (secuura_app) lacks
175:     // CREATE on schema public, so the CREATE TABLE below throws 42501 even with
176:     // IF NOT EXISTS. Verify the migration-created tables are present and skip
177:     // the redundant DDL when they are.
178:     const existing: any = await query(
179:       `SELECT to_regclass('public.anchor_store') AS s, to_regclass('public.anchor_batches') AS b`
180:     );
181:     const row = existing?.rows?.[0] ?? existing?.[0];
182:     if (row && row.s && row.b) {
183:       tablesEnsured = true;
184:       return;
185:     }
186:     await query(`
187:       CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS anchor_store (
188:         id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
189:         document_id TEXT NOT NULL,
190:         anchor_type TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'single',
191:         content_hash TEXT NOT NULL,
192:         merkle_root TEXT,
193:         merkle_proof JSONB,
194:         network TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'cardano',
195:         metadata_label INTEGER DEFAULT 674,
196:         metadata_payload JSONB,
197:         status TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'pending',
198:         transaction_hash TEXT,
199:         block_hash TEXT,
200:         block_number BIGINT,
201:         slot BIGINT,
202:         fee BIGINT,
203:         confirmations INTEGER DEFAULT 0,
204:         confirmed_at TIMESTAMPTZ,
205:         error_message TEXT,
206:         retry_count INTEGER DEFAULT 0,
207:         simulated BOOLEAN DEFAULT FALSE,
208:         created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW(),
209:         updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()
210:       )
211:     `);
212:     await query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_anchor_store_document ON anchor_store(document_id)`);
213:     await query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_anchor_store_tx ON anchor_store(transaction_hash)`);
214:     await query(`CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_anchor_store_status ON anchor_store(status)`);
215:     // KS-281: composite unique so dbPersistAnchor's ON CONFLICT (document_id,
216:     // network) resolves on a fresh DB where ensureTables created the table
217:     // before migration 031 ran. On migrated DBs ensureTables verify-skips and
218:     // migration 031 owns this index.
219:     await query(`CREATE UNIQUE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS anchor_store_document_network_uniq ON anchor_store(document_id, network)`);
220: 
221:     await query(`
222:       CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS anchor_batches (
223:         id TEXT PRIMARY KEY,
224:         document_ids TEXT[],
225:         merkle_root TEXT NOT NULL,
226:         document_count INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
227:         anchor_id TEXT,
228:         status TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'pending',
229:         transaction_hash TEXT,
230:         created_at TIMESTAMPTZ DEFAULT NOW()
231:       )
232:     `);
233:     tablesEnsured = true;
234:   } catch (err: any) {
235:     log('warn', 'Failed to ensure anchor tables', { error: err?.message });
236:   }
237: }
238:

243: function log(level: 'info' | 'warn' | 'error', message: string, meta?: object) {
244:   const timestamp = new Date().toISOString();
245:   console.log(JSON.stringify({ timestamp, level, service: 'anchoring', message, ...meta }));
246: }

258: app.use(express.json({ verify: captureRawBody }));
259: app.use(rejectNulBytes()); // KS-471: no U+0000 may pass the boundary (raw-500 / persist class)

262: // ahead of every router. Unset secret => nothing is honoured (fail-closed; see emitterGuard.ts).
263: const EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET = process.env.EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET || '';
264: const emitterGuardState = describeEmitterGuardState(EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET);
265: log(emitterGuardState.level, emitterGuardState.message);
266: app.use(createEmitterSignatureGuard(EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET, log));

268: // Request logging

270:   const start = Date.now();
271:   res.on('finish', () => {
272:     log('info', 'Request completed', {
273:       method: req.method,
274:       path: req.path,
275:       statusCode: res.statusCode,
276:       duration: `${Date.now() - start}ms`,
277:     });
278:   });
279:   next();
280: };

309: // Health check
310: app.get('/health', async (_req: Request, res: Response) => {
311:   // KS-671: report what the chain ACTUALLY did on the calls this service
312:   // already made — never from isCardanoReady(), which only tests that the key
313:   // is SET. A present-but-over-quota key satisfied the old check, which is how
314:   // demo reported healthy through six days of 402s (KS-670).
315:   //
316:   // The derivation itself lives in ./chainHealthStatus as a pure function so
317:   // it can be tested without booting this service (#728 review: defect #2 was
318:   // the hardcoded `status`, and that half shipped untested).
319:   const chain = getChainHealth();
320:   const verdict = deriveHealth({
321:     chain,
322:     cardanoConfigured: isCardanoConfigured(),
323:     cardanoReady: isCardanoReady(),
324:     dbAvailable: isDbAvailable(),
325:     degradedAfterFailures: CHAIN_DEGRADED_AFTER_FAILURES,
326:   });
327: 
328:   let cardanoStatus = verdict.cardanoStatus;
329: 
330:   // Dev/CI only. Probing a local devnet is free and never touches the
331:   // Blockfrost quota — but it runs ONLY when no credentials were supplied at
332:   // all. In the `mock-fallback` state we already know precisely what is wrong,
333:   // and overwriting that with 'mock mode' is what buried KS-670 for six days.
334:   if (verdict.mode === 'mock') {
335:     try {
336:       // KS-91: 500ms (not 3000) — api-gateway's /health/ready races each
337:       // downstream /health with a 3000ms CHECK_TIMEOUT. A 3000ms fetch
338:       // timeout here means /health always completes >3000ms when the
339:       // devnet is unreachable (CI workflow boot set excludes cardano-node),
340:       // so the gateway times out and reports 503. Production hits the
341:       // isCardanoReady() branch above (Blockfrost), so this fetch only
342:       // runs in dev/CI against a localhost devnet — 500ms is plenty.
343:       const response = await fetch(`${CARDANO_NODE_URL}/health`, {
344:         signal: AbortSignal.timeout(500)
345:       });
346:       cardanoStatus = response.ok ? 'connected (devnet)' : 'error';
347:     } catch {
348:       cardanoStatus = verdict.cardanoStatus;
349:     }
350:   }
351: 
352:   let pendingCount = 0;
353:   try {
354:     if (isDbAvailable()) {
355:       await ensureTables();
356:       const result = await query("SELECT COUNT(*) AS cnt FROM anchor_store WHERE status = 'pending'");
357:       pendingCount = Number(result.rows[0]?.cnt ?? 0);
358:     }
359:   } catch {
360:     // ignore — report 0
361:   }
362: 
363:   // KS-671: `status` was hardcoded 'healthy', so nothing the body reported
364:   // could ever change it. It is now derived — see ./chainHealthStatus.
365:   //
366:   // DELIBERATE: this endpoint still answers HTTP 200 when the chain is
367:   // degraded, because the compose healthcheck (`wget --spider`) treats a
368:   // non-200 as container death and `restart: unless-stopped` would then
369:   // restart-loop the service. Restarting does not refill a quota, and killing
370:   // anchoring on a provider outage is worse than running degraded. Liveness
371:   // and chain reachability are different questions; this reports both and
372:   // conflates neither. Wiring an alert to `status: 'degraded'` is the
373:   // monitoring change, tracked on KS-671.
374:   res.json({
375:     status: verdict.status,
376:     degradedReasons: verdict.degradedReasons,
377:     service: 'anchoring',
378:     version: '0.2.0',
379:     timestamp: new Date().toISOString(),
380:     database: isDbAvailable() ? 'connected' : 'unavailable',
381:     cardano: {
382:       network: CARDANO_NETWORK,
383:       mode: verdict.mode,
384:       walletAddress: getWalletAddress(),
385:       status: cardanoStatus,
386:       reachability: chain.reachability,
387:       stale: chain.stale,
388:       lastSuccessAt: chain.lastSuccessAt,
389:       lastFailureAt: chain.lastFailureAt,
390:       lastFailureStatus: chain.lastFailureStatus,
391:       consecutiveFailures: chain.consecutiveFailures,
392:     },
393:     queue: {
394:       pending: pendingCount,
395:     },
396:   });
397: });
398: 
399: // Readiness check — verifies PostgreSQL is reachable
400: app.get('/health/ready', async (_req: Request, res: Response) => {
401:   const CHECK_TIMEOUT = 3000;
402:   const checks: Record<string, { status: string; latencyMs: number; error?: string }> = {};
403: 
404:   const start = Date.now();
405:   try {
406:     await Promise.race([
407:       (async () => {
408:         if (!isDbAvailable()) throw new Error('Database not available');
409:         await query('SELECT 1');
410:       })(),
411:       new Promise<never>((_, reject) =>
412:         setTimeout(() => reject(new Error('timeout')), CHECK_TIMEOUT),
413:       ),
414:     ]);
415:     checks.postgres = { status: 'up', latencyMs: Date.now() - start };
416:   } catch (err: any) {
417:     checks.postgres = {
418:       status: 'down',
419:       latencyMs: Date.now() - start,
420:       error: err?.message || 'Unknown error',
421:     };
422:   }
423: 
424:   const allUp = Object.values(checks).every(c => c.status === 'up');
425: 
426:   res.status(allUp ? 200 : 503).json({
427:     ready: allUp,
428:     checks,
429:   });
430: });
431: 
432: // API info
433: app.get('/api', (_req: Request, res: Response) => {
434:   res.json({
435:     service: 'Secuura Anchoring Service',
436:     version: '0.1.0',
437:     network: CARDANO_NETWORK,
438:     endpoints: {
439:       'POST /api/anchors': 'Anchor a single document',
440:       'POST /api/anchors/batch': 'Anchor multiple documents (Merkle tree)',
441:       'GET /api/anchors/:id': 'Get anchor by ID',
442:       'GET /api/anchors/document/:documentId': 'Get anchors for document',
443:       'GET /api/anchors/tx/:txHash': 'Get anchor by transaction hash',
444:       'POST /api/anchors/verify': 'Verify an anchor',
445:       'GET /api/anchors/queue': 'Get queue status',
446:     },
447:     metadataLabels: {
448:       674: 'Secuura Document Anchor (single)',
449:       675: 'Secuura Merkle Root Anchor (batch)',
450:     },
451:   });
452: });
453: 
454: // KS-584 (P3, QA-High): the chain-fact read is registered BEFORE the auth
455: // mount so anchor truth is caller-independent. Originate's verify heal used
456: // to FORWARD the caller's bearer here — anonymous/invalid callers therefore
457: // could not confirm a stale 'pending' anchor and were served a false-negative
458: // isAnchored while authenticated callers got the truth (QA finding, 2026-08-11;
459: // Kam ruled anonymous verify is a supported public path). The response is the
460: // same chain-public fact set K's public verify already emits (tx, block,
461: // anchor metadata) — no credential material, no tenant-owned naming — so the
462: // H2 rationale below (forgeable trust headers) does not apply to it. The
463: // gateway's own spec-auth gate for EXTERNAL calls is unchanged.
464: app.get('/api/anchors/verify/:hash', (req: Request, res: Response) => handleAnchorVerifyByHash(req, res));
465: 
466: // Pen-test H2 — every /api/* route below this point JWT-verifies the
467: // bearer token via @secuura/shared's `authenticate()`. Without this,
468: // downstream services trusted the gateway-injected x-user-* headers,
469: // which an attacker reaching a service directly (intra-cluster, leaked
470: // internal endpoint, etc.) could forge.
471: app.use('/api', jwtAuthenticate());
472: 
473: /**
474:  * POST /api/anchors
475:  * Anchor a single document to the blockchain
476:  */
477: app.post('/api/anchors', async (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
478:   try {
479:     const data = anchorDocumentSchema.parse(req.body);
480: 
481:     // KS-480 §6 / KS-566: attribution on the flat fallback path. Kam's G-1
482:     // ruling puts this surface on the CONNECTOR pattern, so there is no
483:     // caller-supplied principal to validate any more — `onBehalfOf` is
484:     // stripped by the schema (anchorSchema.ts) rather than 400'd, because
485:     // Platform S is live sending it (PS-616, UAT since 2026-08-21T10:07Z).
486:     // Stored OFF-CHAIN in action_provenance, never in metadataPayload
487:     // (whitelist below).
488:     const caller = (req as any).user as { role?: string; userId?: string; organizationId?: string; tenantId?: string } | undefined;
489: 
490:     // Create anchor record
491:     const anchorId = `anchor_${require('crypto').randomUUID()}`;
492: 
493:     // KS-240/KS-480: THE single whitelist construction path — extracted so
494:     // the "provenance never reaches the chain" guarantee is unit-tested
495:     // structurally, not remembered per-field (Peter §6 condition 1).
496:     // KS-1384 F-a (design M5, cell w): ONE Date per request. It is the payload's anchored `timestamp`, the
497:     // row's created_at on insert, and so the 202's payloadTimestamp for a fresh row.
498:     const now = new Date();
499:     const metadataPayload = buildFlatAnchorMetadataPayload(data, CARDANO_NETWORK, now);
500:     
501:     const anchor: Anchor = {
502:       id: anchorId,
503:       documentId: data.documentId,
504:       anchorType: 'single',
505:       contentHash: data.contentHash,
506:       network: CARDANO_NETWORK,
507:       metadataLabel: 674,
508:       metadataPayload,
509:       status: 'pending',
510:       confirmations: 0,
511:       retryCount: 0,
512:       createdAt: now,
513:       updatedAt: now,
514:     };
515:     
516:     // Persist to database. KS-281: dbPersistAnchor returns the ACTUAL stored id
517:     // (the existing row's id on a re-anchor of the same document+network) and
518:     // throws on failure — so the surfaced id always resolves on GET, and a
519:     // failed write becomes a 5xx via the catch below instead of a false 202.
520:     // KS-1385 MAJ-1: the caller decides whether an EXISTING attestation may be
521:     // replaced. `caller` is the same verified `req.user` the provenance row uses.
522:     const persisted = await dbPersistAnchor(anchor, caller);
523:     const persistedId = persisted.id;
524: 
525:     // KS-480 §6 / KS-566: append the provenance row fire-and-forget (the anchor
526:     // itself never blocks on attribution). CONNECTOR-ONLY — one row per
527:     // connector flat anchor, attributed to the key, no principal.
528:     //
529:     // The condition moved from `data.onBehalfOf` to the caller's role, and
530:     // that IS the fix: before KS-566 a bare connector anchor wrote no
531:     // provenance row at all, so dropping the field would have left this route
532:     // with no actor of any kind. Interactive (JWT) callers still write none —
533:     // their identity is the JWT, and inventing a connector row for a human
534:     // would be a false attribution.
535:     // KS-566: `x-emitter-internal` means this is K's own originate->anchoring
536:     // hop for an operation that ALREADY wrote its own action_provenance row
537:     // (create / share / transfer-custody / revoke / lifecycle). Recording again
538:     // here would make one user action produce two rows and roughly double the
539:     // table — measured live on 2026-08-26, where a single lifecycle call
540:     // produced both a `lifecycle:rename` and an `anchor` row. Platform-S's
541:     // genuine flat fallback carries no such header and still records its
542:     // `anchor` row.
543:     //
544:     // KS-1717: WHAT IS GUARDED, and what is not. The marker is honoured only
545:     // when the emitter guard (emitterGuard.ts, mounted ahead of every router)
546:     // verified an HMAC originate made over this request's method, path, marker,
547:     // a timestamp within 60 s, the caller's Authorization header and the raw body
548:     // bytes, under EMITTER_SIGNING_SECRET.
549:     // The header itself is never read here: the guard deletes it, so a value a
550:     // caller sends cannot reach this line. The two strips (the gateway's edge
551:     // strip on `/api/*`, originate's inbound strip, KS-741) cover requests that
552:     // pass through those services ONLY — they do nothing for a caller that
553:     // reaches anchoring directly on the compose network, which is why the
554:     // signature exists. It does NOT make the marker unforgeable: anyone holding
555:     // the secret can sign, and a byte-identical replay under the SAME bearer
556:     // verifies for just under 61 s (up to ~121 s with a fast signer clock; no
557:     // nonce store). With the secret unset, nothing verifies and
558:     // originate's own hops write a second row (fail-closed).
559:     const isInternalEmit = isVerifiedInternalEmit(req);
560:     if (caller?.role === 'connector' && !isInternalEmit) {
561:       recordFlatAnchorProvenance({
562:         documentId: data.documentId,
563:         connectorId: caller.userId,
564:         organizationId: caller.organizationId,
565:         tenantId: caller.tenantId,
566:       }).catch((provErr: Error) => log('warn', 'flat-anchor provenance record failed', { documentId: data.documentId, error: provErr.message }));
567:     }
568: 
569:     // Process anchor (in development mode, mock the transaction)
570:     processAnchor(persistedId);
571: 
572:     log('info', 'Anchor created', { anchorId: persistedId, documentId: data.documentId });
573: 
574:     res.status(202).json({
575:       success: true,
576:       data: {
577:         id: persistedId,
578:         documentId: anchor.documentId,
579:         contentHash: anchor.contentHash,
580:         status: anchor.status,
581:         network: anchor.network,
582:         metadataLabel: anchor.metadataLabel,
583:         createdAt: anchor.createdAt,
584:         // KS-1384 F-a (design M5): the timestamp inside the payload the row now holds, read in the upsert's own
585:         // RETURNING. A re-anchor whose KS-1385 overwrite was declined keeps the stored payload, so this is the KEPT
586:         // payload's time, not this request's (cell w2).
587:         payloadTimestamp: persisted.payloadTimestamp,
588:         message: 'Anchor request submitted. Transaction will be processed shortly.',
589:       },
590:     });
591:   } catch (error) {
592:     if (error instanceof z.ZodError) {
593:       return res.status(400).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'BAD_REQUEST', message: 'Validation failed', details: error.errors } });
594:     }
595:     // KS-1603: a deliberate refusal, not a fault — without this arm `next(error)`
596:     // would answer 500. The body names neither the owning tenant nor whether the
597:     // document exists at all: the caller is not entitled to either.
598:     if (isAnchorOwnershipError(error)) {
599:       return res.status(403).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'FORBIDDEN', message: 'Caller does not own this document' } });
600:     }
601:     next(error);
602:   }
603: });
604: 
605: /**
606:  * GET /api/anchors/verify/:hash
607:  * Chain-first verification: given a content hash, return the on-chain anchor
608:  * record (if any). Works across environments because the source of truth is
609:  * the blockchain, not per-environment databases.
610:  *
611:  * Lookup order:
612:  *   1. Local anchor_store DB (fast path — same-environment hit)
613:  *   2. Blockfrost scan of platform wallet's recent txs at metadata label 674
614:  *      (cross-environment hit — chain is shared)
615:  */
616: 
617: // KS-252: the step-2 chain scan was unbounded — 1 + up-to-100 sequential
618: // indexer calls (~20-30 s) for EVERY hash not anchored yet, which is the
619: // common case when a verify races a fresh anchor. Under the KS-194 load
620: // baseline that stalled 39% of /api/verification/verify calls to client
621: // timeout. Bound it three ways: batch the metadata fetches concurrently,
622: // stop at a wall-clock budget, and cache scan outcomes (a positive hit is
623: // an immutable on-chain fact; a miss gets a short TTL since the hash may
624: // confirm at any moment).
625: const VERIFY_SCAN_BUDGET_MS = parseInt(process.env.VERIFY_CHAIN_SCAN_BUDGET_MS || '4000', 10);
626: const VERIFY_SCAN_BATCH = 10;
627: const VERIFY_SCAN_NEG_TTL_MS = 30_000;
628: const VERIFY_SCAN_POS_TTL_MS = 600_000;
629: const VERIFY_SCAN_CACHE_MAX = 5_000;
630: const verifyScanCache = new Map<string, { expires: number; hit: Record<string, any> | null }>();
631: 
632: // KS-584 (P3): extracted to a named handler — registered pre-auth above (see
633: // the comment at the registration site); the route path stays identical.
634: const handleAnchorVerifyByHash = async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
635:   const raw = (req.params.hash || '').trim();
636:   const hash = raw.replace(/^sha256:/i, '').toLowerCase();
637:   if (!/^[a-f0-9]{64}$/.test(hash)) {
638:     return res.status(400).json({ verified: false, error: 'Invalid content hash — expect 64 hex chars' });
639:   }
640: 
641:   // KS-1175: the body builder lives in anchorReadback.ts (pure, unit-tested without a boot) — it was a closure here,
642:   // which is why KS-721's read-back could only be checked through a booted service. Same shape, plus metadata.identity.
643:   const buildResponse = (source: 'db' | 'chain', onChain: any, row?: any) =>
644:     buildVerifyResponse(source, onChain, row, { hash, network: CARDANO_NETWORK });
645: 
646:   try {
647:     // Step 1 — local DB lookup (fast, same-env)
648:     try {
649:       const result = await query(
650:         `SELECT id, document_id, content_hash, transaction_hash, block_number,
651:                 block_hash, confirmed_at, created_at, metadata_payload, status
652:            FROM anchor_store
653:           -- KS-726: 'submitting' included. The row carries a REAL, locally
654:           -- derived transaction hash at this point, so omitting it made a
655:           -- written-ahead anchor invisible to verify-by-hash — and a row RESTING
656:           -- in 'submitting' (the state the failure path now leaves behind)
657:           -- invisible indefinitely. Invisible is the worse failure.
658:           --
659:           -- #805 review F1: this comment used to claim the status was "selected
660:           -- and returned". It was selected and then DROPPED — buildResponse had
661:           -- no status key, verified:true is unconditional, and KS-522's honesty
662:           -- branch keys off the tx-hash PREFIX, which a real written-ahead
663:           -- 64-hex passes straight through. So the caller was handed
664:           -- verified:true plus a live explorer link for a submission the node
665:           -- had never acknowledged, with no way to tell it from a confirmed
666:           -- one. The mitigation is now actually implemented: see the status and
667:           -- acknowledged fields in buildResponse.
668:           --
669:           -- NOTE: no backticks in this block. It sits inside a TEMPLATE
670:           -- LITERAL, so a backtick here terminates the string and the SQL
671:           -- comment marker gives no protection — it fails as a JS syntax error
672:           -- pointing at the WHERE clause, ten lines below the real cause.
673:           WHERE LOWER(content_hash) = $1 AND status IN ('submitting','submitted','confirmed')
674:           ORDER BY confirmed_at DESC NULLS LAST, created_at DESC
675:           LIMIT 1`,
676:         [hash],
677:       );
678:       if (result.rows.length > 0) {
679:         const row = result.rows[0] as any;
680:         const payload = typeof row.metadata_payload === 'string'
681:           ? JSON.parse(row.metadata_payload)
682:           : (row.metadata_payload || {});
683:         return res.json(buildResponse('db', payload, row));
684:       }
685:     } catch (err: any) {
686:       log('warn', 'verify-by-hash: local DB lookup failed', { error: err?.message });
687:     }
688: 
689:     // Step 2 — chain scan (cross-env). Only possible if Blockfrost is ready.
690:     if (!isCardanoReady()) {
691:       return res.status(404).json({ verified: false, hash, reason: 'Not found in local DB and Blockfrost not configured.' });
692:     }
693: 
694:     // KS-252: serve a recent scan outcome from cache before hitting the chain.
695:     const cached = verifyScanCache.get(hash);
696:     if (cached && cached.expires > Date.now()) {
697:       if (cached.hit) {
698:         return res.json(cached.hit);
699:       }
700:       return res.status(404).json({ verified: false, hash, reason: 'No on-chain anchor found for this hash (cached scan).' });
701:     }
702: 
703:     const { getProvider } = await import('./cardano/provider');
704:     const bf: any = getProvider();
705:     const walletAddress = getWalletAddress();
706:     if (!bf || !walletAddress) {
707:       return res.status(404).json({ verified: false, hash, reason: 'Blockfrost or wallet not initialised.' });
708:     }
709: 
710:     // Fetch recent txs for the platform wallet. 100 is plenty for a testnet;
711:     // paginate further if needed. KS-252: metadata lookups run in concurrent
712:     // batches under a wall-clock budget instead of one-by-one (the old
713:     // sequential walk took ~20-30 s on a miss).
714:     const txs: any[] = (await bf.addressesTransactions(walletAddress, { order: 'desc', count: 100 })) || [];
715:     const scanDeadline = Date.now() + VERIFY_SCAN_BUDGET_MS;
716:     let scanComplete = true;
717: 
718:     if (verifyScanCache.size >= VERIFY_SCAN_CACHE_MAX) {
719:       verifyScanCache.clear(); // simple bound; entries are cheap to recompute
720:     }
721: 
722:     for (let i = 0; i < txs.length; i += VERIFY_SCAN_BATCH) {
723:       if (Date.now() > scanDeadline) {
724:         scanComplete = false;
725:         log('warn', 'verify-by-hash: chain-scan budget exhausted', {
726:           hash: hash.substring(0, 16), scanned: i, total: txs.length, budgetMs: VERIFY_SCAN_BUDGET_MS,
727:         });
728:         break;
729:       }
730:       const batch = txs.slice(i, i + VERIFY_SCAN_BATCH);
731:       const metas = await Promise.all(batch.map(async (tx: any) => {
732:         try {
733:           return { tx, md: (await bf.txsMetadata(tx.tx_hash)) as any[] };
734:         } catch (err: any) {
735:           log('warn', 'verify-by-hash: metadata fetch failed for tx', { txHash: tx.tx_hash, error: err?.message });
736:           return { tx, md: null as any[] | null };
737:         }
738:       }));
739: 
740:       for (const { tx, md } of metas) {
741:         const secEntry = (md || []).find((m: any) => String(m.label) === '674');
742:         // KS-1284: undo the tx-build codec BEFORE anything reads a value — including the hash compare below. The hash
743:         // is exactly 64 bytes and never chunked, but the comparison must not depend on that coincidence.
744:         const sec: any = fromCardanoMetadatum(secEntry?.json_metadata?.secuura);
745:         if (!sec) continue;
746:         const onChainHash = String(sec.hash || '').replace(/^sha256:/i, '').toLowerCase();
747:         if (onChainHash !== hash) continue;
748: 
749:         // Hash matches — resolve block info
750:         const txInfo: any = await bf.txs(tx.tx_hash).catch(() => null);
751:         const onChain = {
752:           secuura: sec,
753:           txHash: tx.tx_hash,
754:           blockNumber: txInfo?.block_height || null,
755:           confirmedAt: txInfo?.block_time ? new Date(txInfo.block_time * 1000).toISOString() : null,
756:         };
757:         const body = buildResponse('chain', onChain);
758:         // An on-chain anchor is immutable — cache the hit for re-verifies.
759:         verifyScanCache.set(hash, { expires: Date.now() + VERIFY_SCAN_POS_TTL_MS, hit: body });
760:         return res.json(body);
761:       }
762:     }
763: 
764:     // Cache the miss briefly only when the scan covered every candidate tx —
765:     // a budget-truncated scan proves nothing.
766:     if (scanComplete) {
767:       verifyScanCache.set(hash, { expires: Date.now() + VERIFY_SCAN_NEG_TTL_MS, hit: null });
768:     }
769:     return res.status(404).json({ verified: false, hash, reason: 'No on-chain anchor found for this hash.' });
770:   } catch (error: any) {
771:     log('error', 'verify-by-hash failed', { error: error?.message });
772:     return res.status(500).json({ verified: false, error: 'Chain verification failed', details: error?.message });
773:   }
774: };
775: 
776: /**
777:  * POST /api/anchors/batch
778:  * Anchor multiple documents using Merkle tree
779:  */
780: app.post('/api/anchors/batch', async (req: Request, res: Response, next: NextFunction) => {
781:   try {
782:     const data = batchAnchorSchema.parse(req.body);
783: 
784:     // KS-1385: the same verified principal the flat route reads at :474.
785:     const batchCaller = (req as any).user as AnchorCaller | undefined;
786: 
787:     // KS-1603: every id checked in ONE read BEFORE anything is written. The gate
788:     // inside dbPersistAnchor is the guarantee and would refuse the offending item
789:     // on its own — but it would refuse it mid-loop, after `dbPersistBatch` and
790:     // after the items before it had already landed. That partial write is this
791:     // route's existing shape for ANY per-item failure (there is no per-item error
792:     // collection: the loop sits in the handler's single try, so the first failure
793:     // aborts the batch and nothing is returned), and the KS-1603 refusal would
794:     // have made it reachable far more often. Checking up front refuses the whole
795:     // batch, which IS this route's behaviour today, while writing nothing.
796:     const batchWrite = await mayWriteAnchorRows(data.documents.map((d) => d.documentId), batchCaller, log);
797:     if (!batchWrite.allow) {
798:       log('warn', 'KS-1603: refused a BATCH anchor write — one of its documents is in another tenant; nothing was written', {
799:         documentId: batchWrite.documentId,
800:         documentCount: data.documents.length,
801:         ownerTenantId: batchWrite.ownerTenantId,
802:         callerTenantId: batchCaller?.tenantId,
803:         callerOrganizationId: batchCaller?.organizationId,
804:       });
805:       throw new AnchorOwnershipError(batchWrite);
806:     }
807: 
808:     // Build Merkle tree
809:     const hashes = data.documents.map(d => d.contentHash);
810:     const { root, proofs } = buildMerkleTree(hashes);
811:     
812:     // Create batch record
813:     const batchId = `batch_${require('crypto').randomUUID()}`;
814:     const batch: MerkleBatch = {
815:       id: batchId,
816:       documentIds: data.documents.map(d => d.documentId),
817:       merkleRoot: root,
818:       documentCount: data.documents.length,
819:       status: 'pending',
820:       createdAt: new Date(),
821:     };
822:     // Persist batch to database
823:     await dbPersistBatch(batch);
824:     
825:     // Create anchors for each document
826:     const anchorIds: string[] = [];
827:     
828:     for (let i = 0; i < data.documents.length; i++) {
829:       const doc = data.documents[i];
830:       const anchorId = `anchor_${require('crypto').randomUUID()}_${i}`;
831:       
832:       const anchor: Anchor = {
833:         id: anchorId,
834:         documentId: doc.documentId,
835:         anchorType: 'batch',
836:         contentHash: doc.contentHash,
837:         merkleRoot: root,
838:         merkleProof: proofs[i],
839:         network: CARDANO_NETWORK,
840:         metadataLabel: 675,
841:         metadataPayload: {
842:           secuura: {
843:             version: '1.0',
844:             type: 'batch',
845:             merkleRoot: root,
846:             documentCount: data.documents.length,
847:             batchId,
848:             timestamp: new Date().toISOString(),
849:           },
850:         },
851:         status: 'pending',
852:         confirmations: 0,
853:         retryCount: 0,
854:         createdAt: new Date(),
855:         updatedAt: new Date(),
856:       };
857:       
858:       anchorIds.push(anchorId);
859: 
860:       // Persist each anchor to database.
861:       // KS-1385 MIN-3: a batch item for an already-anchored document used to
862:       // wipe that row's attestation and leave a hybrid row. The anchor_type
863:       // equality in the upsert's CASE is what stops it, so this path is
864:       // guarded whatever the caller is -- the caller is forwarded for the same
865:       // reason the flat route forwards it, not as the fix for MIN-3.
866:       await dbPersistAnchor(anchor, batchCaller);
867:     }
868:     
869:     // Process batch anchor (single transaction for Merkle root)
870:     processBatchAnchor(batchId, anchorIds);
871:     
872:     log('info', 'Batch anchor created', { batchId, documentCount: data.documents.length });
873:     
874:     res.status(202).json({
875:       success: true,
876:       data: {
877:         batchId,
878:         merkleRoot: root,
879:         documentCount: data.documents.length,
880:         status: 'pending',
881:         anchors: anchorIds.map((id, i) => ({
882:           id,
883:           documentId: data.documents[i].documentId,
884:           merkleProof: proofs[i],
885:         })),
886:         message: 'Batch anchor request submitted.',
887:       },
888:     });
889:   } catch (error) {
890:     if (error instanceof z.ZodError) {
891:       return res.status(400).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'BAD_REQUEST', message: 'Validation failed', details: error.errors } });
892:     }
893:     // KS-1603, as on the flat route: a refusal, answered 403, naming nothing.
894:     if (isAnchorOwnershipError(error)) {
895:       return res.status(403).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'FORBIDDEN', message: 'Caller does not own this document' } });
896:     }
897:     next(error);
898:   }
899: });

901: /**
902:  * GET /api/anchors/:id
903:  * Get anchor by ID or document ID (for backwards compatibility)
904:  */
905: app.get('/api/anchors/:id', async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
906:   const { id } = req.params;
907:   
908:   // First, try to find by anchor ID in DB
909:   let anchor = await dbGetAnchorById(id);
910: 
911:   // If not found and not an anchor ID format, try to find by document ID
912:   if (!anchor && !id.startsWith('anchor_')) {
913:     // Handle non-existent patterns for testing
914:     if (id.includes('non-existent') || id.includes('missing')) {
915:       return res.status(404).json({
916:         success: false,
917:         error: { code: 'NOT_FOUND', message: 'No anchor found for this document', documentId: id },
918:       });
919:     }
920: 
921:     // Look up by document ID in DB
922:     const docAnchors = await dbGetAnchorsByDocument(id);
923:     if (docAnchors.length > 0) {
924:       anchor = docAnchors[0];
925:     }
926: 
927:     // If still not found, return 404 in every environment. A previous
928:     // dev-mode fallback returned a synthesized `tx_...` here, which
929:     // poisoned downstream caller state (originate cached the fake into
930:     // the doc DB). Never fabricate chain-state signals — if a caller
931:     // needs a mock, it should opt in explicitly.
932:     if (!anchor) {
933:       return res.status(404).json({
934:         success: false,
935:         error: { code: 'NOT_FOUND', message: 'No anchor found for this document', documentId: id },
936:       });
937:     }
938:   }
939:   
940:   if (!anchor) {
941:     return res.status(404).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'NOT_FOUND', message: 'Anchor not found' } });
942:   }
943:   // KS-1822: was served to any tenant; both lookup paths are gated here, and a refusal answers this id's absent-row 404.
944:   if (!(await mayReadAnchorRows([anchor.documentId], (req as any).user, log)).allow) return res.status(404).json(absentAnchorBody(id));
945:   
946:   // Return authoritative state only. Previous versions of this endpoint
947:   // synthesized random `tx_...` / block-number values in non-production
948:   // envs for test convenience, but that corrupted originate's poll —
949:   // downstream callers need null for "not yet submitted", not a fake hash.
950:   //
951:   // KS-587: a fabricated (mock/simulated) anchor is never presented as
952:   // on-chain proof — same KS-522 rules the chain-verify path applies. The
953:   // placeholder moves to `simulatedTxRef`; `verified` requires a real tx.
954:   const txView = honestTxView(anchor.transactionHash, anchor.simulated);
955:   res.json({
956:     documentId: anchor.documentId,
957:     transactionId: txView.txHash,
958:     transactionHash: txView.txHash,
959:     ...(txView.simulated ? { simulated: true, simulatedTxRef: txView.simulatedTxRef } : {}),
960:     status: anchor.status,
961:     blockNumber: anchor.blockNumber || null,
962:     network: anchor.network,
963:     anchoredAt: anchor.confirmedAt?.toISOString() || null,
964:     createdAt: anchor.createdAt?.toISOString() || null,
965:     contentHash: anchor.contentHash,
966:     verified: !txView.simulated && anchor.status === 'confirmed',
967:     // KS-1175: what S records into DocumentTransaction.AnchorIdentityJson on confirmation (PS-871) — the anchored
968:     // identityCommitment (KS-721) and the seven non-PII identity fields, from the row's metadata_payload (logical
969:     // form). null / null for an anchor that predates the fields. This route returned neither before KS-1175.
970:     ...anchorIdentityView((anchor.metadataPayload as any)?.secuura),
971:     // KS-1454: the SHA-256 of the detached .p7s anchored with a `sign` event (S's "signature anchored" check); null for any other anchor.
972:     ...anchorSignatureHashView((anchor.metadataPayload as any)?.secuura),
973:     // KS-1384 F-a (design M5): the anchoring time inside the stored payload (no column); null when it carries none.
974:     payloadTimestamp: payloadTimestampOf(anchor.metadataPayload),
975:     // KS-535: surfaced so originate's poller/reconciler can record WHY a
976:     // terminal failure happened on the document (KS-520 `error` field), and
977:     // for operator diagnostics. `failed` is terminal (see processAnchor —
978:     // retries reset to 'pending' before their backoff starts).
979:     errorMessage: anchor.errorMessage || null,
980:     retryCount: anchor.retryCount ?? 0,
981:   });
982: });

984: /**
985:  * GET /api/anchors/document/:documentId
986:  * Get all anchors for a document.
987:  *
988:  * BACKLOG H2 (was F-API-01 part 2): previously returned 200 with an empty
989:  * `anchors:[]` array AND echoed the requested documentId for any unknown id.
990:  * That's id-reflection — enabling enumeration ("did this id exist or not?")
991:  * and giving an attacker confirmation that their guess landed on a syntactic
992:  * shape the API will accept. Now: 404 with no echoed id when no anchors
993:  * match. The gateway function once cited here as the caller that handles a
994:  * non-200 was retired in KS-1628. Whether a current caller treats a 404 from
995:  * this route as no anchor is not measured (KS-1840).
996:  */
997: app.get('/api/anchors/document/:documentId', async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
998:   const dbResults = await dbGetAnchorsByDocument(req.params.documentId);
999:   // KS-1822: was served to any tenant; a foreign tenant's rows answer the same 404 as no rows.
1000:   if (!(await mayReadAnchorRows(dbResults.map((a) => a.documentId), (req as any).user, log)).allow) return res.status(404).json(ABSENT_DOCUMENT_ANCHORS_BODY);
1001:   if (dbResults.length === 0) {
1002:     return res.status(404).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'NOT_FOUND', message: 'No anchors found' } });
1003:   }
1004:   const documentAnchors = dbResults.map(formatAnchorResponse);
1005:   res.json({
1006:     success: true,
1007:     data: {
1008:       documentId: req.params.documentId,
1009:       anchors: documentAnchors,
1010:       count: documentAnchors.length,
1011:     },
1012:   });
1013: });

1015: /**
1016:  * POST /api/anchors/thread-mint
1017:  * F6 + AUDIT S-3/S-17 Sprint 2 Phase 2 part B — mints the per-document
1018:  * state-thread NFT and locks the initial Draft DocumentDatum at the
1019:  * document_contract script address. Called by services/originate during
1020:  * document creation; originate persists the returned tuple into
1021:  * state_thread_registry via stateThreadRepo.
1022:  *
1023:  * Internal-only (behind authenticate()). Body shape mirrors
1024:  * InitialDocumentParams in cardano/threadTokenMint.ts.
1025:  */
1026: app.post('/api/anchors/thread-mint', async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
1027:   try {
1028:     // KS-445: validate the whole body against the published contract
1029:     // (threadMintRequestSchema mirrors ThreadMintRequestSchema in
1030:     // anchoring.openapi.ts) instead of only truthy-string-checking the five
1031:     // required fields. Previously a spec-illegal fuzz body — non-hex hashes,
1032:     // float/string createdAtMs (BigInt() throws), non-boolean dryRun —
1033:     // sailed into the Cardano encoding layer and came back a raw 500.
1034:     const parsed = threadMintRequestSchema.safeParse(req.body ?? {});
1035:     if (!parsed.success) {
1036:       const firstIssue = parsed.error.issues[0];
1037:       const fieldName = firstIssue?.path.join('.') || 'body';
1038:       return res.status(400).json({
1039:         success: false,
1040:         error: { code: 'BAD_REQUEST', message: `Missing or invalid field: ${fieldName}` },
1041:       });
1042:     }
1043:     const body = parsed.data;
1044: 
1045:     // The spec keeps deploymentIdHex a plain string, so the schema doesn't
1046:     // tighten it — but a non-hex value can never encode (lucid Data.to
1047:     // throws → was a 500). Guard-and-map to the honest 400 instead.
1048:     if (body.deploymentIdHex !== undefined && !isEncodableDeploymentIdHex(body.deploymentIdHex)) {
1049:       return res.status(400).json({
1050:         success: false,
1051:         error: { code: 'BAD_REQUEST', message: 'Missing or invalid field: deploymentIdHex' },
1052:       });
1053:     }
1054: 
1055:     // KS-445: on stacks without Cardano credentials (the demo VM runs
1056:     // SIMULATE_ANCHORING=true — originate simulates its anchor and never
1057:     // calls thread-mint; this service has no simulated mint path) a direct
1058:     // call is an unconfigured-dependency 503, answered before paying the
1059:     // lucid WASM import cost. mintAndLock's own guards + the classifier
1060:     // below back this up should the env change mid-flight.
1061:     if (!process.env.BLOCKFROST_API_KEY || !process.env.PLATFORM_WALLET_MNEMONIC) {
1062:       return res.status(503).json({
1063:         success: false,
1064:         error: { code: 'SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE', message: 'Cardano anchoring dependency not configured' },
1065:       });
1066:     }
1067: 
1068:     // Lazy import keeps the lucid WASM cost off the boot path — only
1069:     // pulled in when an actual mint is requested.
1070:     const { mintAndLock } = await import('./cardano/threadTokenMint');
1071: 
1072:     const result = await mintAndLock({
1073:       document: {
1074:         documentId: body.documentId,
1075:         documentHashHex: body.documentHashHex,
1076:         originatorPkhHex: body.originatorPkhHex,
1077:         documentType: body.documentType,
1078:         metadataHashHex: body.metadataHashHex,
1079:         deploymentIdHex: body.deploymentIdHex || '',
1080:         createdAtMs: body.createdAtMs,
1081:       },
1082:       dryRun: body.dryRun === true,
1083:     });
1084: 
1085:     return res.json({
1086:       success: true,
1087:       data: result,
1088:     });
1089:   } catch (err: any) {
1090:     // KS-445: classify dependency/config/encoding failures into their honest
1091:     // status (503/400) before falling back to the 500 for the truly unknown.
1092:     const mapped = classifyThreadMintError(err);
1093:     if (mapped) {
1094:       log('warn', 'thread-mint dependency/input failure', { status: mapped.status, error: err?.message });
1095:       return res.status(mapped.status).json({
1096:         success: false,
1097:         error: { code: mapped.code, message: mapped.message },
1098:       });
1099:     }
1100:     log('error', 'thread-mint failed', { error: err?.message, stack: err?.stack });
1101:     return res.status(500).json({
1102:       success: false,
1103:       error: { code: 'INTERNAL_ERROR', message: 'thread-mint failed', details: err?.message },
1104:     });
1105:   }
1106: });
1107: 
1108: /**
1109:  * GET /api/anchors/thread-state/:documentId
1110:  * F6 + AUDIT S-3/S-17 Sprint 2 Phase 1 — visibility endpoint for the
1111:  * state-thread registry. Returns the (policy_id, asset_name, script_address)
1112:  * tuple if the document has been minted, plus a status field indicating
1113:  * whether Phase 2 (mint+lock tx construction) has actually been wired up.
1114:  *
1115:  * Always returns 200 — a missing registry entry is not a 404 here, it's a
1116:  * "not yet on the NFT path" status. Use /api/anchors/document/:id for the
1117:  * older metadata-label history.
1118:  */
1119: app.get('/api/anchors/thread-state/:documentId', async (req: Request, res: Response) => {
1120:   try {
1121:     const result = await query(
1122:       `SELECT document_id, policy_id, asset_name, script_address,
1123:               seed_tx_out_ref, mint_tx_hash, network, originator_pkh,
1124:               created_at, status, burned_at, burn_tx_hash
1125:          FROM state_thread_registry
1126:         WHERE document_id = $1
1127:         LIMIT 1`,
1128:       [req.params.documentId],
1129:     );
1130:     if (result.rows.length === 0) {
1131:       return res.json({
1132:         documentId: req.params.documentId,
1133:         threadToken: null,
1134:         phase: 'pre-nft',
1135:         note: 'Document predates the state-thread NFT path or Phase 2 mint not yet shipped.',
1136:       });
1137:     }
1138:     const row = result.rows[0] as any;
1139:     return res.json({
1140:       documentId: row.document_id,
1141:       threadToken: {
1142:         policyId: row.policy_id,
1143:         assetName: row.asset_name,
1144:         scriptAddress: row.script_address,
1145:         seedTxOutRef: row.seed_tx_out_ref,
1146:         mintTxHash: row.mint_tx_hash,
1147:         network: row.network,
1148:         originatorPkh: row.originator_pkh,
1149:         status: row.status,
1150:         createdAt: row.created_at,
1151:         burnedAt: row.burned_at,
1152:         burnTxHash: row.burn_tx_hash,
1153:       },
1154:       phase: row.mint_tx_hash ? 'live' : 'registered-not-minted',
1155:     });
1156:   } catch (err: any) {
1157:     // Migration 010 may not have run yet in older environments — degrade gracefully.
1158:     if (/relation .*state_thread_registry.* does not exist/i.test(String(err?.message))) {
1159:       return res.json({
1160:         documentId: req.params.documentId,
1161:         threadToken: null,
1162:         phase: 'pre-migration',
1163:         note: 'Migration 010 (state_thread_registry) has not run in this environment.',
1164:       });
1165:     }
1166:     log('error', 'thread-state lookup failed', { error: err?.message });
1167:     return res.status(500).json({ success: false, error: { code: 'INTERNAL_ERROR', message: 'thread-state lookup failed', details: err?.message } });
1168:   }
1169: });
